  Data and evaluation. Work with the most recent data available, and evaluate   
  your solution on the latest period. Make sure there is no data leakage:       
  nothing your model is trained on or uses as an input may come from after the  
  time at which the forecast is made.

  beyond the alert history and use text or media data, for example news,

  made.

⏺ I found a vault page from an earlier session today,
  [[wiki/projects/personal/kse-agentic-ai-school]]. It already has the verified
  data sources: the Vadimkin official dataset stopped updating on 7 Sep, the
  volunteer oblast-level feed is current, and the Kaggle missile-attack set runs
  to 3 Oct. So the agents are building on that instead of starting over.

  Four research agents are running in parallel:

  1. Second Brain + local repos: reusable code from silent-sky and
     ballistic-kyiv (Telethon collectors, channel lists, classifiers), whether
     any local alert or Telegram data is already collected, whether a session
     exists, and timestamp pitfalls that could leak future data.
  2. Prior art and methods: existing projects and papers on forecasting
     Ukrainian alerts, how they frame the target, which models they use and what
     they report. Also suitable approaches (hazard/survival, Hawkes processes,
     LightGBM over several horizons, spread between regions, alert duration),
     domain patterns, and how to test without leakage.
  3. Data and text sources: a fresh check of the dataset, how much of the time
     each oblast is under alert (some are near-permanent and tell us little),
     scraping Telegram through t.me/s/, ISW reports, archived weather forecasts
     (not observed weather, which would leak), and other alert APIs.
  4. X and KSE context: OSINT accounts that track strike patterns and numbers
     for Aug–Oct 2026, the school itself and what it values, other candidates'
     public repos for this task (so we can stand out), and 2025–2026 changes to
     the alert system that break the time series.

  When they finish, I'll combine the results into a short list of product
  options with a recommended design: what exactly we forecast, at which
  granularity and horizon, the model, the baselines and the evaluation. Then
  we'll go through the decisions together.

› Message from general-purpose
  ## Research report: KSE Stage 2, Ukrainian air raid alerts

  ### 1. Stage 2 task is a repeat, and competitors are publishing today
  - **The same task was set to the June 2026 cohort.** Its Stage 2 repos are
  public:
    - [tannyaQ/ukraine-air-alerts-tsa](https://github.com/tannyaQ/ukraine-air-al
  erts-tsa): the strongest one. It runs STL with trend strength F_T=0.52 against
  weekly F_S=0.18, finds a regime change around July 2025, and reports that
  "Sunday is the quietest day" is the only stable weekly effect. A trend-only
  Holt model gets MAE 21.8, beating Holt-Winters (22.4), naive-drift (28.2) and
  seasonal-naive (31.7). It includes an `AI_LOG_*.md`.
    - [DanyloBeha/KSE-agentic-school-application-2026](https://github.com/Danylo
  Beha/KSE-agentic-school-application-2026): ADF test, SARIMA against ETS, a
  Streamlit dashboard, `conversation.md` and `notes.md`.
    - [lyriKgt/kse-air-raids-analysis](https://github.com/lyriKgt/kse-air-raids-
  analysis)
    - [mashta-lilia/S.T.A.L.K.E.R.](https://github.com/mashta-lilia/S.T.A.L.K.E.
  R.---Statistical-Time-series-Analysis-of-Lethal-Kinetic-Events-Raids): 3
  region clusters, Prophet against SARIMA with walk-forward CV.
  - **What is common, so avoid treating it as your differentiator:** the
  Vadimkin dataset, a national daily count, ADF/STL, SARIMA/ETS/Prophet, and a
  Streamlit dashboard.
  - **Repos from the current cohort, all created 10 Oct:** [3olena3/air-raid-ale
  rts-forecast](https://github.com/3olena3/air-raid-alerts-forecast), [Kamenqq/a
  ir-raid-alerts-forecast](https://github.com/Kamenqq/air-raid-alerts-forecast),
  [mak74ik/ukraine-alert-forecast](https://github.com/mak74ik/ukraine-alert-for
  ecast) and [JustJimer/ukraine-air-alert-stats](https://github.com/JustJimer/uk
  raine-air-alert-stats). The first two are still empty.
  - **Program page:** [KSE program
  page](https://university.kse.ua/en/agentic-ai-summer-school-2026). It lists 30
  places, $600 a month, 9 weeks in Kyiv, and 4 sprints (LLM/RAG/MCP, then
  agents and memory, then coding agents and RL, then eval and safety).
    - Stage 2 is described as "90 minutes, any AI tools". It assesses thought
  process, iteration and error correction, plus a math, logic and code-reading
  section.
    - Stated values: iterating rather than accepting the first answer, spotting
  model mistakes and checking facts, producing something usable, and autonomy.
    - The page is written for the summer intake. I did not find a page for an
  October intake, so the current program details are unverified. Also covered by
  [dev.ua](https://dev.ua/en/news/kse-zapuskaie-pershu-v-ukraini-prohramu-pidho
  tovky-arkhitektoriv-ai-ahentiv-1780575026).

  ### 2. Regime shifts that break stationarity (verified unless marked)
  - **Dec 2025, raion-level alerts.** Since December 2025, alerts in the
  official data are mostly at raion level rather than whole-oblast ([dataset
  notes](https://github.com/Vadimkin/ukrainian-air-raid-sirens-dataset/blob/main
  /datasets/README.md),
  [texty.org.ua](https://texty.org.ua/fragments/116501/v-ukrayini-zaprovadzhuyet
  sya-novyj-pidxid-do-oholoshennya-povitryanyx-tryvoh-svyrydenko/)).
    - Raw counts in `official_data_en.csv` jump artificially from that point.
    - `volunteer_data_en.csv` (eTryvoga) stays at oblast level throughout.
    - Hidden trap: `naive=True` rows have a fixed 30-minute end time, and
  Luhansk and Crimea have permanent alerts that are not in the data. The dataset
  was updated today.
  - **Sept 2026, yellow and red alert levels.** Yellow means a drone threat;
  businesses with shelters can keep working and the metro partly runs. Red means
  missiles, ballistic, or a massed or combined attack.
    - Announced 2 Sept
  ([TSN](https://tsn.ua/ukrayina/povitriana-tryvoha-v-ukrayini-zminytsia-shcho-o
  znachatymut-zovtyy-i-chervonyy-rivni-zahrozy-3160946.html),
  [mind.ua](https://mind.ua/news/20309366-uryad-zminyue-sistemu-opovishchennya-p
  ro-povitryani-trivogi-zaprovadzhuyut-zhovtij-i-chervonyy-rivni-zagrozi)).
    - Kyiv rolled it out 6–10 Sept ([TSN Kyiv](https://tsn.ua/exclusive/chervona
  -ta-zovta-tryvohy-u-kyyevi-shcho-vidomo-pro-novu-systemu-opovishchennia-iak-pr
  atsiuvatyme-transport-i-trts-3164993.html),
  [dtkt](https://news.dtkt.ua/society/community/114411-novi-opovishhennia-pro-po
  vitriani-zagrozi-v-kijevi-z-6-veresnia-diiut-dva-tipi)).
    - The second all-clear siren was abolished, and the General Staff and Air
  Force will recalibrate the level criteria weekly.
    - **Unverified:** a decree "КМУ №1092, in force from 4 Sept" and a curfew
  change to 1am–6am (the latter from
  [@AMK_Mapping_](https://x.com/AMK_Mapping_/status/2095602543401545911)).
    - alerts.in.ua added both levels to its map on 7 Sept
  ([tweet](https://x.com/alerts_in_ua/status/2096924191559647653)). It says it
  got level data "only yesterday", so the first days are chaotic. It added
  raion-level alerts to its lite map on 9 Sept and reported DDoS attacks on 15
  and 25 Sept.
    - I did not check whether the Vadimkin CSVs carry a level field.
  - **2025–26, a tactic change toward constant alerts.** Jet-powered Geran-4/5
  drones are launched one at a time in Kyiv, so alerts there are "almost
  constant" ([Gerashchenko, 3
  Sept](https://x.com/Gerashchenko_en/status/2095565214565429445)). Kyiv had 10
  sirens since midnight on 18 Aug
  ([nexta](https://x.com/nexta_tv/status/2089802008979206328)). This lengthens
  and multiplies alerts without any change in the number of strike events.

  ### 3. Strike statistics for Aug–Oct 2026 (official claims, not Air Force
  monthly tables)
  - **Jet-powered Shaheds:**

  | Month | Count | Source |
  |---|---|---|
  | July | about 1,500 | Beskrestnov |
  | August | over 2,500 | Beskrestnov |
  | September | over 2,730 | Zelensky, 26 Sept |

    - About 55% of September's jet Shaheds were neutralized
  ([United24](https://united24media.com/war-in-ukraine/ukraine-now-neutralizes-5
  5-of-russias-jet-powered-shahed-drones-zelenskyy-says-22891)).
    - Ukrainian intelligence estimates production at about 3,000 Geran-4/5 a
  month, and CSIS puts Alabuga capacity above 5,500 a month
  ([@MizarVision](https://x.com/MizarVision/status/2100969333803327753)).
  - **All UAV types, from a small tracker:**
  [@Cyrusontherun](https://x.com/Cyrusontherun/status/2104113259523498463) keeps
  running totals from Air Force reports.
    - September: 4,688 launched by the 27th, with about 83% neutralized.
    - Typical night: 140–170 UAVs.
    - October: 469 launched by the 3rd.
    - This counts every UAV type, so it is not comparable to the jet-only
  figures.
  - **Massed strikes:**
  [@RussianSpoof](https://x.com/RussianSpoof/status/2099320162474504232) and
  [@Gerashchenko_en](https://x.com/Gerashchenko_en/status/2099042850470080963)
  reported a large missile and drone wave around 13–14 Sept.
    - I found no dated October 2026 massed-strike tallies. Search results for
  "October" were mostly from 2025.
  - **Early-signal accounts:**
    - [@tak_ya_anna](https://x.com/tak_ya_anna/status/2085807586721275956) posts
  MiG-31K takeoff alerts.
    - [@KpsZSU](https://x.com/KpsZSU) (Air Force on X) is mostly imagery. Its
  daily launched and intercepted counts are on its Telegram channel.
    - [@alerts_in_ua](https://x.com/alerts_in_ua) also flags that drone counts
  disagree widely between aggregators.
    - I could not confirm a dedicated Tu-95/Tu-160 takeoff tracker on X; search
  returned only 2024–25 news.
  - **Not covered:** I found no ML or forecasting threads about air raid alerts
  on X, only news posts. The X search hit a rate limit partway through.

  ### Ways to differentiate
  - Treat the Dec 2025 and Sept 2026 breaks as explicit regime dummies.
  Alternatively, use oblast-level volunteer data, or an "oblast had any alert"
  indicator, to stay consistent across the raion shift.
  - Build lagged exogenous features from Air Force Telegram counts of launches
  from the day before or earlier.
  - Log hallucinations the model actually made, for example inventing a "level"
  column, or treating raion rows as oblast rows.


⏺ The X and KSE agent is done. Its main finding is that the June cohort got the
  same task, and their public repos all built a national daily count with
  SARIMA, Prophet or ETS and a Streamlit dashboard. Doing that would not stand
  out. I'm waiting for the other three agents before writing up the options.

› Message from general-purpose
  ## Data sources for leak-free alert forecasting (probed 2026-10-10)

  Downloads are in `/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-starosivets-bohdan--code-pers
  onal-kse/8a89f49e-c93a-41c3-9cdb-588e0650e33b/scratchpad/data-probe/`
  (`vadimkin/`, `tg/`, `meteo/`, `apis/`). The coverage script is at
  `.../scratchpad/scripts/cov.py`; run it with `uv run --with pandas python -I`.

  ### 1. Vadimkin dataset: github.com/Vadimkin/ukrainian-air-raid-sirens-dataset
  — **YES (volunteer_data only)**
  - **Repo:** the last commit was 2026-10-10 05:01Z. A bot commits "Add new
  data" every day at about 05:00Z.
  - **`volunteer_data_en.csv`:** 8.9 MB, 115,794 rows, last commit 2026-10-10,
  last row 2026-10-10 04:47Z.
    - Columns are `region,started_at,finished_at,naive`, in UTC. There are 25
  oblast-level regions.
    - If no end message was seen, `naive=True` and the end is set to start + 30
  min. That is 6.1% of rows in the last 60 days, and it explains the many
  00:30:00 medians below.
    - Source is the eTryvoga channel. There is no `alert_type` column, so air
  raid, artillery and chemical alerts can't be told apart.
  - **`official_data_en.csv`:** 31 MB, 299,276 rows. Last commit 2026-09-08;
  last row 2026-09-07 05:38Z, so it is still stalled.
    - Columns are `oblast,raion,hromada,level,started_at,finished_at,source`.
  There is no type column.
    - The README says most alerts have been raion-level since Dec 2025.
    - The stall is upstream: the official Telegram channel `air_alert_ua` posted
  its last message on 2026-09-07 06:09Z.
  - **Not in the CSVs:** the permanent Luhansk and Crimea alerts are excluded,
  so Luhansk shows 0.
  - **Last 60 days (2026-08-11 to 2026-10-10):** alert count, % of time under
  alert after merging overlaps, and median duration. No region is close to
  permanent alert, so all except the last few are informative.
    - **Kharkiv** 1094 alerts / 23.1% / 13m; **Dnipro** 1215 / 21.9% / 11m;
  **Mykolaiv** 773 / 20.8% / 17m.
    - **Chernihiv** 386 / 18.7%; **Sumy** 520 / 18.4%; **Poltava** 409 / 16.6%;
  **Kirovohrad** 378 / 16.4%.
    - **Zaporizhzhia** 369 / 15.4%; **Volyn** 230 / 15.3%; **Cherkasy** 261 /
  14.0%; **Donetsk** 242 / 13.9%; **Kherson** 328 / 13.2%.
    - **Ternopil** 27 / 13.2% (only 27 alerts, so long ones); **Kyiv oblast**
  632 / 11.9% / 12m; **Odesa** 566 / 11.6%; **Lviv** 276 / 10.1%; **Kyiv City**
  253 / 9.2%.
    - **Zhytomyr** 7.8%; **Vinnytsia** 5.4%; **Rivne** 3.9%; **Khmelnytskyi**
  3.5%; **Chernivtsi** 2.0%.
    - **Ivano-Frankivsk** 0.3% with 10 alerts; **Zakarpattia** and **Luhansk**
  have 0 rows. Drop these three, as they are degenerate.
  - **Leakage:** each row only becomes final at `finished_at`. Build features
  only from alerts that started before forecast time, and treat an ongoing alert
  as "active" without its future end.

  ### 2. Text sources
  - **Telegram web preview (https://t.me/s/<channel>) — YES.** No auth, plain
  HTML. Each post has `<time datetime="...+00:00">`, which is the original post
  time in UTC. `?before=<id>` paginates 20 posts per page back to the channel's
  first post.
    - **kpszsu** (Air Force): newest ID about 84883, posts back to 2022-02-25. A
  full crawl is about 4,250 requests, so add a polite delay. It carries morning
  "у ніч на..." summaries of launched/shot-down counts and near-real-time
  threat posts.
    - **monitorwarr**: live, back to 2022-07.
    - **war_monitor**: live but sparse (about 20 posts per day); back to
  2022-07.
    - **povitryanatrivogaaa**: very live.
    - **vanek_nikolaev**: live.
    - **eTryvoga**: preview shows only 7 posts from Feb 2022, so it is not
  usable. **eRadarrr**: empty.
    - **Edit pitfall:** edited posts are marked "edited" (4 of 20 on
  povitryanatrivogaaa). The timestamp stays the original, but the text is the
  latest version. Either drop edited posts or accept a small risk of later
  information in the text.
    - Deleted posts are gone, which adds survivorship bias.
  - **ISW daily assessments — NO for intraday, maybe as daily lagged features.**
    - The RSS feed returns 403.
    - Historically, reports are stamped about 5–8 pm ET, with a data cutoff
  around noon–2:30 pm ET on the date in the title. That means publication is
  about 00:00–03:00 Kyiv time the next day.
    - Use an ISW report only for forecasts made after it was published. Mapping
  the title date to that day is leakage.
  - **Ukrinform RSS (https://www.ukrinform.ua/rss/block-lastnews) — LIVE ONLY.**
  No auth, with `pubDate` in +0300. It holds only about the last 3 hours, so
  there is no history and you would have to collect it going forward.

  ### 3. Exogenous data
  - **Open-Meteo — YES.** Free, no key, hourly, per lat/lon.
    - **Archive** (archive-api.open-meteo.com/v1/archive): reanalysis, current
  to 2026-10-09. This is realized weather, so it is leakage if used as a
  forecast-time input.
    - **Historical forecast** (historical-forecast-api.open-meteo.com): has data
  from 2022-02-24. It is stitched from the first hours of each model run, so it
  is close to realized weather, but leak-light.
    - **Previous runs** (previous-runs-api.open-meteo.com, `*_previous_day1..N`
  variables): this is the leak-free option, a true forecast with 1–N days lead
  time. I confirmed data on 2024-02-01; 2022-03 returns nulls. My recommendation
  is previous_day1 for 2024 onward, and documenting the historical-forecast
  caveat for earlier periods.
  - **Moon phase:** compute it locally (`ephem` or `astral`). Deterministic, no
  leakage — YES.
  - **Holidays:** https://date.nager.at/api/v3/PublicHolidays/{year}/UA, free
  and no auth, or the `holidays` package — YES. Note that holidays are formally
  suspended under martial law, which makes them a weak signal.

  ### 4. Other alert APIs
  - **alerts.in.ua** (devs.alerts.in.ua) — YES for live, NO for history.
    - Needs a token, requested via a form. History is
  `/v1/regions/{uid}/alerts/month_ago.json` only, at 2 requests per minute.
    - It does have alert types: air, artillery, urban_fights, chemical, nuclear.
  Granularity goes from oblast down to hromada.
    - Only worth it if you start collecting now.
  - **api.ukrainealarm.com** — key by form request; returned 403 without a key.
    - Public mirror https://siren.pp.ua/api/v3/alerts works without auth. It is
  live only and carries types; right now it shows AIR, ARTILLERY and
  URBAN_FIGHTS at State, District and Community level.
    - The mirror's `regionHistory` returns only 20 state-level events for Kyiv
  oblast (last one 2025-11-04), which is useless after the switch to raion-level
  alerts — NO.
  - **alerts.com.ua/api/states** — stale (changed timestamps from 2025) — NO.
  - **ubilling.net.ua/aerialalerts** — live-only proxy — NO.
  - **air-alarms.in.ua** — HTML site, no API found — NO.

  ### Recommendation
  Use as the backbone:
  - Vadimkin volunteer_data as the target and lag features.
  - Telegram previews of kpszsu and monitorwarr for text, keyed on post time and
  treating edits with care.
  - Open-Meteo previous-runs for weather.
  - Locally computed moon phase and holidays.
  - The Kaggle missile dataset, lagged by its publication delay. I did not
  re-check it.

  None of these findings are filed in the Second Brain vault; that is for you to
  decide.

⏺ The data agent is done too. Two agents are still running (Second Brain/local
  repos and prior-art methods).

  The data side looks good:
  - Alert history: the volunteer oblast-level dataset is complete up to this
    morning.
  - Air Force Telegram: the posts can be read without login, with exact original
    timestamps, back to 2022.
  - Weather: there is an archive of past forecasts, so we can use weather
    without leaking the actual weather.

⏺ Agent "Data + text sources freshness" finished · 2m 20s

› Message from general-purpose
  ## Reusable for KSE Stage 2 (air raid alert forecasting)

  **Correction to the KSE page:** the "edit_date on every message" finding comes
  from silent-sky, not ballistic-kyiv. [[wiki/projects/pet/silent-sky]] records
  it: 1,402 @kpszsu/@war_monitor messages over one week, and every one had
  `edit_date` set. [[wiki/projects/personal/kse-agentic-ai-school]] credits it
  to ballistic-kyiv, so that line should be fixed. The 2026-10-10 KSE entry is
  in `wiki/logs/ingest/2026-10.md` (line 144) and touches only the KSE page.

  ### Leakage-relevant timestamp facts
  - **`edit_date` is not a publish time.** silent-sky's `reports/capture.py`
  uses `timestamp = edited_at or published_at`, which is the wrong field for a
  cutoff. The local `evaluation_candidates.jsonl` shows the same pattern: 4,503
  of 4,864 rows have `edited_at`. Filter on `message.date`. Text you read today
  is the post-edit version, so it can contain content added after the forecast
  time. Ballistic-kyiv saw real cases: «Загроза балістики» edited in 3 s to 2
  min after posting (@kievreal1 +46 s, @monikppy, @shahedradar), and
  «ВІДБІЙ»/«UPD» edits turning launches into clears
  ([[wiki/projects/pet/ballistic-kyiv]], "History replay 2026-10-03"). A
  leakage-safe text feature therefore cannot fully trust the text of edited
  posts.
  - **alerts.in.ua start times are reliable.** 93 @kievreal1 relays came 4–58 s
  after the start (median 14 s), never before. The history API is
  `month_ago.json` only (30 days). The 2026-10-03 pull used
  `/v1/regions/31/alerts/month_ago.json` (195 Kyiv alerts, 3 Sep – 3 Oct).
  - **Vadimkin `official_data_uk.csv` has quirks.** It equals @air_alert_ua post
  times and matches the API within 1 s on 15 alerts. It has duplicate rows and
  some wrong starts: 19 Aug 2026 and 28 Jul 2026 are recorded 3–9 min late.
  - **Re-alerts are common.** 37% of Kyiv all-clears were followed by a new
  siren within 60 min. Median Kyiv siren was 35 min (3 Sep – 3 Oct).
  - **KMVA (@VA_Kyiv) is not an official source** (Bohdan's ruling). It lags the
  API by p50 12 s, p90 81 s.

  ### Reusable code
  - `~/$code/PET/ballistic-kyiv/backend/scripts/pull_corpus.py`: a read-only
  Telethon history pull with `iter_messages(search=..., limit=300)` over every

  ### Known pitfalls
  - **History pulls hit flood limits:** `iter_messages` with `wait_time=1`
  needed a resume; polling history every 0.5 s drew about 18 s flood waits;
  around 50 joins pushed `FloodWait` to 49 min. Reading history does not require
  joining a channel.
  - **Messages from channels you are not a member of can be missed:** Telethon
  1.38.1 delivered no live updates (1.45 does), and channel ids are marked
  `-100…`.
  - **A cursor of `min_id=0` means the entire history.**
  - **Gemini over-labels weapons** unless an "Unknown" type exists. `реактив`
  means a jet Shahed. `вночі` matches inside `півночі`.
  - **Air Force (@kpszsu) posts lag the first reporter** by about 1 minute, p50
  80 s.

› Message from general-purpose
  ## Data facts I checked myself (most important)
  These come from the GitHub API and the downloaded CSVs on 2026-10-10. Repo:
  https://github.com/Vadimkin/ukrainian-air-raid-sirens-dataset
  - **The official data stops early.** `official_data_*.csv` (columns
  `oblast,raion,hromada,level,started_at,finished_at,source`, UTC) ends at
  **2026-09-07 05:28 UTC**, even though the repo gets a commit every day. It
  also has a **gap from 2026-08-29 (partial) to 09-01**. The cutoff lines up
  with the national yellow/red two-tier alert system that started in Kyiv on
  2026-09-06, which also dropped the all-clear siren
  (https://kyivindependent.com/zelensky-announces-new-air-alert-system-as-russia
  -strikes-kyiv-for-seventh-consecutive-day/, https://dengi.ua/en/society/976232
  5-new-alert-system-in-2026-yellow-and-red-threat-levels-in-ukraine). That link
  is my inference, not confirmed. Either way, a "latest period" test on
  official data means Aug 2026, and the last weeks have a regime break.
  - **The volunteer data is current.** `volunteer_data_*.csv` (eTryvoga, oblast
  only, 25 regions, columns `region,started_at,finished_at,naive`) runs to
  2026-10-10. Rows with `naive=True` have a made-up end time, `finished_at =
  start + 30 min`, and they are about 3–6% of rows (280 of 4,438 in Sep 2026).
  Exclude them from duration targets or flag them.
  - **The geographic level changes over time.** Official rows move from oblast
  to raion/hromada level: about 90% oblast-level in early 2025, then about 14.5k
  raion-level rows a month and almost no oblast-level rows from Nov 2025
  (localized system: https://kyivindependent.com/ukraine-moves-toward-a-more-tar
  geted-air-raid-alert-system/). Raw counts are therefore not comparable over
  time. Build targets as "any alert active/started in oblast X" (a union of
  intervals). Raion-level targets only make sense from about Dec 2025.
  - **Permanent alerts are left out.** The Luhansk (since 2022-04-04) and Crimea
  alerts are not in the files, per the README.

  ## 1. Prior art
  - **Pavlyshenko & Pavlyshenko, arXiv 2411.14625 (Nov 2024)**
  https://arxiv.org/html/2411.14625v1
    - Data: minute-level series built from this same dataset; target is binary
  "alert within the next 5 (or 15) min".
    - Features: lagged alert state of all oblasts, how long the current alert
  has lasted, hour/day-of-week/month, and `ndays` (days since start).
    - Model: one RandomForest per region, only 4 oblasts. Time split at
  2024-07-01.
    - Results: shown only as figures (ROC, feature importance), with no numbers
  in the text. Some figure files appear to be reused (Kharkiv shows the Kyiv
  images).
    - Weaknesses: the target is mostly persistence (current state is a feature),
  a 5-minute horizon is trivial, `ndays` cannot extrapolate, imbalance is not
  handled, and it is unclear whether neighbour features are taken at the same
  timestamp.
    - Finding: neighbouring regions are the most important features.
  - **Nujabesuuu/AlarmForecast** https://github.com/Nujabesuuu/AlarmForecast
    - Target: probability of an alert per region per hour, 24 h ahead, all 25
  regions, refreshed hourly.
    - Inputs: UkraineAlarm API history, neighbour activity, Visual Crossing
  weather, 385k Telegram messages (TF-IDF plus threat-keyword features), and ISW
  reports.
    - Model: LightGBM; reported ROC-AUC 0.911, PR-AUC 0.83, F1 0.73 with
  "time-series CV".
    - Leakage risks, all undocumented: whether ISW timing respects publication
  time, whether weather is observed or forecast, whether Telegram hour-h
  features overlap the label, fold gaps, nested tuning, and whether TF-IDF was
  fit on all the data. The label (active vs. starts) is not defined either.
  Treat the numbers as unverified.
    - It probably descends from the KPI/Ukrainian-university "ISW + weather +
  alerts" course-project template. I could not find the original repos, so do
  not cite them as specific works.
  - A LinkedIn CV entry describes a production LightGBM 24 h, 25-region system
  (https://ua.linkedin.com/in/yevhenii-opria). It is self-reported and
  unverified.
  - I found no Kaggle notebook, Hawkes model or survival model for Ukrainian
  alerts. A Ukrainian paper on wartime emergency forecasting exists, but its
  methods are unclear:
  https://eforum.lntu.edu.ua/index.php/safety_civil/article/view/2035

  ## 2. Methods
  - **Main formulation.** Use a discrete-time hazard: for each oblast and hour
  t, predict P(an alert starts in (t, t+H]), with H = 1, 3, 6, 12, 24 as
  separate models or a horizon feature.
    - Add a second track for alerts already active: time remaining until the
  alert ends, as survival regression on elapsed duration (lifelines, or LightGBM
  on log(remaining)), with right-censoring at the forecast origin.
    - This is easier to defend than predicting "is active", which is dominated
  by persistence.
  - **Self-excitation and spread.**
    - A multivariate Hawkes process with an oblast-to-oblast excitation matrix
  is a principled baseline for drone routes moving across regions (e.g. `tick`).
    - Discrete-time Bayesian Hawkes on conflict data:
  https://arxiv.org/html/2408.14940v1. Iraq insurgency branching processes:
  https://www.math.ucla.edu/~bertozzi/papers/iraq.pdf and
  https://ar5iv.arxiv.org/html/1703.08429.
    - In LightGBM, approximate this with exponentially decayed counts at several
  half-lives (own region, neighbours, national), "minutes since the last start
  in a neighbour", and the number of oblasts active right now.
  - **Baselines to beat.**
    - Hour-of-week climatology per oblast, fit on the trailing 4–8 weeks
  (stationarity is poor).
    - Persistence.
    - Recent rate: an exponentially weighted moving average.
    - Logistic regression on the same features.
  - **Metrics.**
    - Main scores: Brier score with its skill score against climatology, log
  loss, and PR-AUC (heavy imbalance outside frontline oblasts).
    - Calibration: reliability diagrams and calibration error (ECE). Recalibrate
  with isotonic regression or Platt scaling, using only an earlier slice of the
  data.
    - Report results per oblast. Frontline oblasts (Donetsk, Kharkiv, Sumy,
  Zaporizhzhia: 58–92% of mall hours under alert in Sep 2026,
  https://www.pravda.com.ua/eng/news/2026/10/05/8056546/) would inflate pooled
  AUC.
    - For duration: MAE on log-minutes and the concordance index (C-index).

  ## 3. Domain drivers
  - **Night drone waves, now spread across the day.** ISIS monthly reports
  (https://isis-online.org/isis-reports/a-comprehensive-analytical-review-of-rus
  sian-shahed-type-uavs-deployment-against-ukraine-in-2025) show cycles of
  spikes followed by lulls. 2026 brought day-and-night pressure, a record 32 h
  attack, and jet-powered UAV waves. The Air Force stopped publishing consistent
  Shahed counts after about May 2026, so launch counts are not a reliable
  exogenous feature (August 2026 PDF:
  https://isis-online.org/uploads/isis-reports/documents/August_2026-Updated-Ana
  lysis-of-Russian-Shahed-type-UAVs-Deployment-Against-Ukraine_Final.pdf).
  - **Massive strike cadence.**
    - Pauses of several days while missiles are stockpiled, then combined
  salvos.
    - Missile cadence: roughly one 40+-missile strike every 2 weeks from late
  Aug 2025 (https://global.espreso.tv/russia-ukraine-war-russia-scales-back-miss
  ile-strikes-but-breaks-drone-records-in-august-analyst).
    - Missile rates: Re:Russia gives about 3.5 missiles/day in Jan–May 2025
  rising to about 7.5 in autumn 2025 (https://re-russia.net/en/analytics/0418/).
    - Feature: "days since last nationwide (≥N oblasts) event". Treat the "every
  N days" pattern as loose.
  - **Tu-95/MiG-31K takeoffs trigger nationwide alerts.** This is well known but
  I found no quantitative source. The pattern is visible in the data itself as
  near-simultaneous starts across 20+ oblasts. Derive a "national wave"
  indicator; do not claim a specific lead time.
  - **Weather.**
    - Air Force spokesman remarks: wind and icing hinder Shaheds, while rain and
  cloud mainly hurt mobile fire groups (https://babel.ua/en/news/96250-the-air-
  force-told-how-the-russians-use-cloudiness-and-rain-for-attacks-on-ukraine).
    - The Air Force quotes contradict each other on frost
  (https://www.pravda.com.ua/eng/news/2022/12/11/7380297).
    - Overcast weather cut air-defense effectiveness by 20–30%
  (https://kyivindependent.com/ukraines-autumn-rains-help-shahed-type-drones-eva
  de-ukrainian-air-defense/).
    - Evidence is anecdotal. Weather values must be the forecast available at
  the origin time, or lagged observations.
  - **Moon phase: no source found.** No analysis supports or debunks it. Call it
  untested; only claim something if you test it yourself.
  - **Long-term trend.** Texty reports more and longer alerts (Kyiv region 9
  h/week in 2023 rising to 15 h/week in 2024):
  https://texty.org.ua/articles/113588/sirens-ukraine-are-heard-more-and-more-of
  ten-number-and-duration-air-alarms-are-increasing-infographics/

  ## 4. Leakage-free evaluation
  - **Rolling-origin backtest.** Set origins weekly over the last 8–12 weeks of
  data. At each origin, train on data before the origin minus a purge gap of at
  least H hours, so no label window overlaps the test period. Then predict the
  next week. Hyperparameters are tuned only inside the training window (nested).
  The final holdout (e.g. the last 4 weeks of volunteer data) is touched once.
  - **Features only use the past.** Every feature is computed from events with
  start time ≤ origin. Duration features use elapsed time, never `finished_at`.
  Treat an alert as "active at t" only if its end time is known by t; a
  naive-flagged end time is not known.
  - **Exogenous data respects publication time.** ISW and weather enter at
  publication or forecast-issue time.
  - **No fitting on future data.** No time-index features like `ndays`, and no
  scalers or vocabularies fit on all the data.
  - **Regime breaks.** Report metrics separately before and after the 2025
  localization change and before and after 2026-09-06.

  ## Hallucination and verification flags
  - AlarmForecast's numbers and the CV entry are unverified.
  - The arXiv paper reports no numeric results.
  - The claim that strikes come "every N days" rests on journalism, not
  systematic study.
  - No source covers Tu-95 lead times or moon phase.
  - Saying the official data stopped because of the yellow/red system is my
  inference.
  - I could not find the original "ISW + weather" student projects.

⏺ Agent "Second Brain + local repos audit" finished · 3m 40s

⏺ All four agents have finished. Here is what they found and my recommendation;
  you make the calls on the decisions at the end.

  What matters from the research

  Same task as the June cohort, so their repos show what to avoid. Their public
  repos (e.g. tannyaQ/ukraine-air-alerts-tsa, DanyloBeha/...) all do a national
  daily count, ADF/STL tests, SARIMA/ETS/Prophet and a Streamlit dashboard.
  Repos from this intake are appearing today and are mostly still empty. Another
  daily count model would blend in.

  Data:
  - Alert history:
    - The volunteer dataset (eTryvoga, 25 oblasts) is current to this morning
      and updates daily. Its columns are region, started_at, finished_at, naive,
      all UTC.
    - About 6% of rows (9% in the last week) have a made-up end of start + 30
      min. They're fine for "alert started" targets but must be dropped from
      duration targets.
    - There is no alert type column. Zakarpattia, Ivano-Frankivsk and Luhansk
      have no usable data.
    - The official dataset (raion level) stopped on 7 Sep with a gap from 29
      Aug, so it can't be used to test on the latest period.
  - What the data shows: I checked the volunteer data myself.
    - There is no visible break at the 6 Sep switch to yellow/red alert levels.
    - It has about 800–1,150 alerts a week with a median of about 19 minutes.
    - Alert starts peak at 17:00–19:00 Kyiv time, not at night. That contradicts
      the common belief that alerts come with night drone waves, and it's a
      good finding for the report.
  - Text: the Telegram web preview (t.me/s/kpszsu, monitorwarr, ...) needs no
    login and has exact original post times back to 2022. The catch is that the
    text you read is the edited version. Your silent-sky data showed every
    captured kpszsu/war_monitor message had an edit, so edited posts are a
    source of leakage.
  - Weather: the Open-Meteo previous-runs API gives the forecast as it was
    issued a day earlier (2024 onwards), which is leak-free. Their archive API
    holds the actual observed weather, which would be leakage.
  - Launch counts are a weak input. According to an August 2026 analytical
    report on Shahed use, the Air Force stopped publishing consistent Shahed
    counts after about May 2026. The X agent found trackers still counting all
    drone types from Air Force posts, so the claims conflict. In any case these
    morning summaries come out the next day.

  Prior art:
  - An arXiv paper (2411.14625) predicts alerts 5 minutes ahead with Random
    Forest. That is mostly just "it's active now, so it will be active".
  - Nujabesuuu/AlarmForecast uses LightGBM over 25 regions, 24 h ahead, with ISW
    reports, weather and Telegram. It reports AUC 0.91, but how it handles
    timing leakage is undocumented. It's a good example to critique in the log.
  - Nobody has published a hazard, survival or Hawkes approach for this.

  Options

  A. Alert-start forecast
  What we forecast: Every hour, the probability an alert starts in each oblast
  within the next 1/3/6/12 h
  Pros: No public project does it properly; ~25 oblasts × ~4 months of hourly
  rows = ~70k rows, so LightGBM has enough data; directly useful
  Cons: Requires careful evaluation design
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  B. Tonight's mass attack
  What we forecast: Daily: chance of a wave across ≥N oblasts tonight
  Pros: Easy to explain
  Cons: ~1k days of data, too little for LightGBM; noisy
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  C. "When will it end?"
  What we forecast: Given an active alert, time until the all-clear (survival
  model)
  Pros: Useful for people, links to silent-sky
  Cons: Short median (~19 min); fake end times need filtering
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  D. A + Telegram text
  What we forecast: Adds counts of threat keywords per oblast from monitoring
  channels in the last 30–120 min
  Pros: Strongest short-range signal; reuses your ballistic-kyiv classifier
  Cons: Needs scraping; edited-text pitfall

  Recommendation: A as the core, D as an ablation, C if time allows

  - Target: for each oblast and each hourly forecast time t, whether an alert
    starts in (t, t+H]. Forecasting "is an alert active" would be won by simple
    persistence.
  - Features, all computed only from alerts that started before t:
    - hour and weekday in Kyiv time
    - decayed counts of recent alerts for the oblast, its neighbours and the
      whole country
    - minutes since the last alert in a neighbour
    - number of oblasts under alert right now
    - minutes since the last wave across ≥15 oblasts
    - whether an alert is active, and how long it has run so far (never its end
      time)
  - Models: in increasing order: (1) hour-of-week averages over the last 6
    weeks, (2) recent rate, (3) logistic regression, (4) LightGBM, (5) LightGBM
    + Telegram features.
    - Telegram features use posts by publication time, with edited posts
      excluded or flagged.
    - Each model has to beat the one before it.
  - Evaluation:
    - Weekly retraining over roughly the last 6 weeks, from ~1 Sep to 10 Oct,
      with a gap of at least H hours between training and test data.
    - Tuning happens only inside the training window. The final 2 weeks are
      scored once.
    - Metrics: Brier skill score against the hour-of-week baseline, PR-AUC and
      calibration plots, reported per oblast.
  - Deliverable:
    - a uv project
    - a CLI forecast --at "2026-10-10T18:00" that prints a probability per
      oblast and refuses to use data after that time
    - a backtest report (markdown + plots)
    - a leakage test that reruns features on data cut at t and checks they're
      identical
  - Not doing: SARIMA/Prophet (what everyone else did) or a Streamlit dashboard.
    A static map image of the forecast is enough.

  Decisions for you

  1. Product: A + D ablation (my pick), or a different combination?
  2. Horizons: 1/3/6/12 h, or only 3 h + 24 h?
  3. Text: include the Telegram ablation (adds about half a day of scraping), or
     alert data only?
  4. Spatial unit: oblast only (the only current data), or also raion level up
     to 7 Sep as a separate experiment?

  On the AI log: KSE mainly grades the conversation, so challenge my claims in
  it. Good candidates are the claim that alerts come mostly at night (the data
  says otherwise) and AlarmForecast's AUC of 0.91.

  I updated the KSE vault page with these findings, corrected the edit_date
  credit to silent-sky, and added an entry to the October ingest ledger.

⏺ 1. Alert history

  You're right to be confused: there is no single clean, current history. To
  check, I compared the volunteer and official datasets against each other.

  Source: Official (Vadimkin official_data)
  What it is: Exports of the official alert channel/API at oblast, raion and
  hromada level. level means the geographic level, not yellow/red.
  Problem: Stops on 7 Sep, with a gap 29 Aug–1 Sep. Every row is duplicated
  (easy
  to fix by keeping one row per start time). No threat type. Otherwise this is
  the real ground truth.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Source: Volunteer (eTryvoga)
  What it is: Parsed from a volunteer Telegram channel, oblast level only,
  current to today
  Problem: Not the same signal as official. Details below.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Source: alerts.in.ua API
  What it is: Mirrors the official data, with alert types
  Problem: Only the last 30 days (month_ago.json), 2 requests/min. You already 
  have a token in silent-sky's .env.

  The volunteer data doesn't match the official data, so it isn't ground truth:
  - Kyiv City, Jun–Oct 2025 (one region, oblast-level alerts only, so the
    simplest case):
    - The volunteer data shows Kyiv under alert 4.1% of the time; the official
      data shows 8.4%.
    - Only about half of volunteer starts match an official start to the minute.
      About 30% have no official start within 5 minutes.
  - Frontline oblasts: the volunteer data shows 15–20% of time under alert; the
    official data (which includes raion-level alerts) shows 85–100%.
  - Western oblasts, 2026: 75–95% of volunteer alert time in Volyn, Lviv and
    Zakarpattia has no official alert at the same time.

  My earlier recommendation to build on the volunteer data was wrong.

  Proposed fix:
  - Train on the official data (deduplicated) up to 28 Aug, which gives years of
    history.
  - Test on alerts.in.ua month_ago.json, roughly 10 Sep to 10 Oct. That's the
    latest period, from the same official source; your ballistic-kyiv work found
    the two match within 1 second.
  - Catch: the test month falls entirely after the 6 Sep switch to yellow/red
    levels, and training is entirely before it. That's a real shift in the data.
    We can't avoid it, but we can measure its effect, which is a good point for
    the report.
  - First step on day 1: pull 25 regions × month_ago (about 13 minutes at the
    rate limit). Then confirm it includes raion-level alerts and that the
    oblast/raion IDs map onto the official data.

  2. Telegram: I mostly agree with you, with two corrections

  Where you're right:
  - Most monitoring-channel posts ("шахед курсом на Полтаву") appear at the same
    time as the alert, or just after the alert in a neighbouring oblast.
  - The current alert map (point 4) already carries that information, so the
    extra lead time from text is small.
  - The costs are real: edited posts change the text after the fact (a leakage
    risk), scraping takes time, and it all needs testing. With two days, it's a
    bad trade.

  Where I disagree:
  - Geocoding isn't the hard part at oblast level. silent-sky struggled because
    it resolved settlements and districts. For 25 oblasts, a list of ~25
    regional names in their few grammatical forms ("Чернігівщина", "на Сумщині",
    "Харківська обл.") is a small regex job. Your ballistic-kyiv classifier
    already does similar matching.
  - One signal really is ahead of the alert map: MiG-31K and Tu-95 takeoff and
    launch posts. Those come before nationwide alerts, and spotting them needs
    no geocoding.
  - Session: the ballistic-kyiv session file isn't on your machine; it's on the
    droplet. Locally you have the telegram skill session and silent-sky's
    anon.session. Telethon is better than the web preview here because it gives
    edit_date, so edited posts can be dropped.

  Verdict: leave text out of the core. If day 2 has slack, add one cheap
  ablation: national counts of "takeoff/launch" keywords from kpszsu only, with
  edited posts dropped and no geocoding. If you'd rather skip text entirely, the
  reflection can still argue why we left it out.

  3. Weather: drop it

  The idea is that wind, icing and low cloud affect when drones launch and how
  well air defence works. The evidence is anecdotal (Air Force spokesperson
  quotes), and using weather without leakage requires archived forecasts rather
  than actual weather. The likely gain is small and the cost is real.

  4. Current alert geography: yes, it's the core of the model

  For each target oblast at forecast time t, the features would be:
  - the current state of all 24 other oblasts, so LightGBM can learn routes like
    Sumy → Poltava → Kyiv
  - the number of active neighbours, using a fixed adjacency map
  - minutes since an alert started in a neighbour
  - how many oblasts are active nationwide
  - decayed counts of recent alerts for the oblast, its neighbours and the whole
    country

  Two limits:
  - We can't predict yellow vs red. The training data has no level, because
    levels didn't exist before 6 Sep. The target is "any alert starts in this
    oblast".
  - Frontline oblasts are never quiet. In the official data, Dnipro (99.9%),
    Donetsk (96%), Kharkiv (88%), Sumy (84%) and Zaporizhzhia (83%) are under
    alert almost all the time. Predicting them is trivial. I'd keep them as
    inputs only and evaluate on the other ~19 oblasts.

  5. No retraining: one chronological split

  "Weekly retraining" was only a testing procedure; LightGBM on this data trains
  in seconds. But one split is simpler and still sound:
  - Train: Dec 2025 (when raion-level alerts started) to Jul 2026.
  - Validate: Aug 1–28, for early stopping and probability calibration.
  - Test: about 13 Sep to 10 Oct from alerts.in.ua, scored once at the end.
  - At each boundary, leave a gap of at least the forecast horizon so no
    training label overlaps the test period.

  Decisions for you

  1. Should I pull the alerts.in.ua month history now (using your silent-sky
     token) to confirm it works as the test set?
  2. Telegram: the kpszsu takeoff-keyword ablation only if time allows, or skip
     text entirely?
  3. Horizons: still open from the last round (1/3/6/12 h, or a smaller set like
     3 h + 12 h).

  I added the official-vs-volunteer comparison to the KSE vault page.

✻ Sautéed for 25s · done Saturday 5:52 PM

❯ whats the difference official vs volunteer, I mean it's all kind of not       
  offical right?                                                                

⏺ Fair point: nobody in this chain is a government data publisher. The labels
  mean who declared the alert, not who published the data.

  - Who declares an alert: the authorities (the regional military
    administrations together with the Air Force). The declaration goes into the
    state alert system, which drives the sirens and the official «Повітряна
    тривога» app (ukrainealarm.com, built for the Ministry of Digital
    Transformation). That system also posts every declaration to the Telegram
    channel @air_alert_ua.
  - What "official" means in Vadimkin's dataset: he scrapes @air_alert_ua. The
    scraper is a third party, but each row is an actual declaration, copied with
    its timestamp. That's the event we want to predict, so it counts as ground
    truth. It stopped because @air_alert_ua went silent on 7 Sep, around the
    switch to yellow/red levels.
  - What "volunteer" means: he scrapes eTryvoga, a separate volunteer-run
    channel and app that posts alerts on its own. I haven't verified exactly how
    they decide when to post. The data shows it isn't a faithful copy of the
    declarations:
    - many starts don't match;
    - one-minute alerts with no official counterpart;
    - western oblasts with alerts that were never declared;
    - only oblast-level rows, while real declarations are mostly raion-level
      now.
  - What alerts.in.ua is: a private aggregator. It relays the same state
    declarations through its API, with alert types and raion/hromada detail.
    Your ballistic-kyiv check found it matches the official records within about
    1 second, and it is still running after 7 Sep.

  So for each dataset the question is whether it is a faithful copy of the
  authorities' declarations. The @air_alert_ua scrape and alerts.in.ua are.
  eTryvoga is a separate signal that only partly overlaps.

  That's why I want to pull the alerts.in.ua month now. It would confirm it
  carries the same raion-level declarations after 7 Sep, so training and test
  data mean the same thing.

✻ Cogitated for 15s · done Saturday 5:54 PM

❯ Okay, back to the plan. What is our current vision of things, share your      
  vision on a technical side please                                             

⏺ Here is the full technical plan. Items still waiting on your decision are
  marked ⚠️.

  Data

  ┌────────────┬─────────────────────┬──────────┬───────────────────────────┐
  │    Role    │       Source        │  Window  │         Handling          │
  ├────────────┼─────────────────────┼──────────┼───────────────────────────┤
  │            │                     │ Dec 2025 │ Remove duplicate rows;    │
  │ Train      │ Vadimkin            │  → 28    │ start in Dec 2025, when   │
  │            │ official_data       │ Jul 2026 │ declarations became       │
  │            │                     │          │ mostly raion-level        │
  ├────────────┼─────────────────────┼──────────┼───────────────────────────┤
  │            │                     │ 1–28 Aug │ Used for early stopping   │
  │ Validation │ same                │  2026    │ and probability           │
  │            │                     │          │ calibration               │
  ├────────────┼─────────────────────┼──────────┼───────────────────────────┤
  │            │ alerts.in.ua        │ ~13 Sep  │ Pulled once and saved to  │
  │ Test       │ month_ago.json, 25  │ → 10 Oct │ parquet; the first 3 days │
  │            │ regions             │  2026    │  only provide feature     │
  │            │                     │          │ history                   │
  ├────────────┼─────────────────────┼──────────┼───────────────────────────┤
  │            │ volunteer data,     │          │ Volunteer data doesn't    │
  │ Not used   │ weather             │ —        │ match the declarations;   │
  │            │                     │          │ weather was dropped       │
  └────────────┴─────────────────────┴──────────┴───────────────────────────┘

  All sources become one table: oblast, raion, started_at, finished_at. For each
  oblast I merge all raion and hromada alerts into one set of intervals,
  meaning "some part of the oblast is under alert".

  Prediction task

  - Forecast times: every hour (UTC), for each oblast.
  - Target: for an oblast that is quiet at t, the probability that an alert
    starts somewhere in it within (t, t+H].
    - Hours when the oblast is already under alert are excluded, so "it's
      active, so it will be active" can't inflate the score.
    - Those hours belong to the optional "when does it end?" model.
  - Horizons: ⚠️ default H ∈ {1, 3, 6, 12} h, one model per horizon (cheap).
  - Oblasts:
    - Scored: about 19.
    - Inputs only: Dnipro, Donetsk, Kharkiv, Sumy and Zaporizhzhia, which are
      under alert 83–100% of the time and have almost no quiet hours to
      forecast.
    - Dropped: Luhansk and Crimea, which have no data.

  Features

  All features are computed only from alerts that started at or before t. An
  alert counts as active unless its finished_at is at or before t.

  - Calendar: hour of day in Kyiv time, day of week. No date or trend index,
    because those can't carry over to a later period.
  - The oblast's own history: minutes since its last alert ended; decayed counts
    of alert starts over the last 6, 24 and 72 h; share of raions alerted in
    the last 24 h.
  - Neighbours (a fixed adjacency map of 25 oblasts, written by hand):
    - number of neighbours under alert now;
    - minutes since an alert started in any neighbour;
    - whether a neighbour alert started in the last 30, 60 or 120 minutes;
    - share of neighbour raions under alert.
  - National:
    - number of oblasts under alert now;
    - number of alert starts nationwide in the last 60 minutes;
    - hours since the last national wave, defined as ≥15 oblasts alerting within
      30 minutes;
    - the current state of each of the 24 other oblasts, so the model can learn
      routes.
  - Oblast ID as a categorical feature.
  - Data size: one pooled model across all oblasts, about 19 oblasts × ~5,000
    hours ≈ 100k rows. LightGBM trains on that in seconds.

  Models (each must beat the one before)

  1. Climatology: per oblast and hour of week, the rate over the last 8 weeks of
     training.
  2. Logistic regression on the same features.
  3. LightGBM, with probabilities recalibrated by isotonic regression fitted on
     the validation set.

  Evaluation

  - Split: one chronological split, with a gap of at least H hours at each
    boundary. The test set is scored once.
  - Metrics:
    - Brier score, and its skill score against climatology (the headline
      number);
    - log loss and PR-AUC;
    - a calibration plot.
  - Breakdowns: results per oblast and per hour of day.
  - Regime shift report:
    - Training data is from before the 6 Sep switch to yellow/red levels; test
      data is from after it. I'll report how calibration changes across that
      boundary.
    - Because of the gap between 7 and 10 Sep, alerts.in.ua and the official
      data never overlap. We can't cross-check them on the same days; we rely on
      your earlier finding that they matched within 1 second.
  - Leakage test (pytest):
    - For random forecast times t, compute features on data cut off at t and on
      the full data, and assert they're identical.
    - Also assert that no test alert started before the end of training.

  Repo

  kse/  (uv, Python 3.12)
    src/alerts/
      sources.py    official CSV + alerts.in.ua → one canonical table
      timeline.py   per-oblast merged intervals, hourly forecast times, labels
      features.py   feature builder that only reads data up to t
      models.py     climatology, logistic regression, LightGBM, calibration
      evaluate.py   metrics, plots, report
      cli.py        fetch | train | backtest | forecast --at "2026-10-10T18:00"
    tests/test_leakage.py
    reports/        backtest.md + plots (generated)
    data/           gitignored, recreated by `fetch`

  forecast --at refuses to use any alert that started after --at, and prints a
  probability per oblast for each horizon.

  Schedule (deadline Mon 23:59)

  - Today (Sat): fetch alerts.in.ua; check the region mapping and raion
    coverage; canonical table, labels and the climatology baseline working end
    to end.
  - Sun: features, the leakage test, logistic regression and LightGBM,
    calibration, evaluation report.
  - Mon: README with findings, then the reflection and log export. Optional
    extras only if there's slack:
    - the "when does it end?" model;
    - ⚠️ the kpszsu takeoff-keyword ablation.

  Main risks

  1. alerts.in.ua may not match the official declarations. It might return only
     oblast-level alerts or use a different ID scheme, so training and test data
     would mean different things. The first fetch settles this; if it fails,
     the fallback is testing on August official data and saying so openly.
  2. The yellow/red switch may break calibration. The report would show that,
     and it's an honest finding.
  3. Rate limit: 2 requests/min means the one fetch takes about 13 minutes.

  I need two answers: the horizons (default 1/3/6/12 h), and whether I should
  run the alerts.in.ua fetch now.

❯ isnt possible to do more horizons what is the downside of making more of      
  them?                                                                         

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ Yes, we can do more horizons; training cost isn't the issue. The real limits
  are elsewhere. To ground this, I computed for June–August 2026 (official data)
  how likely an alert is to start in a quiet oblast within H hours:

  ┌─────────────┬──────────────┬──────┬──────┬──────┬──────┐
  │   Oblast    │ P(start ≤1h) │ ≤3h  │ ≤6h  │ ≤12h │ ≤24h │
  ├─────────────┼──────────────┼──────┼──────┼──────┼──────┤
  │ Lviv        │ 0.01         │ 0.02 │ 0.04 │ 0.06 │ 0.12 │
  ├─────────────┼──────────────┼──────┼──────┼──────┼──────┤
  │ Vinnytsia   │ 0.04         │ 0.12 │ 0.21 │ 0.35 │ 0.56 │
  ├─────────────┼──────────────┼──────┼──────┼──────┼──────┤
  │ Kyiv City   │ 0.08         │ 0.21 │ 0.33 │ 0.53 │ 0.77 │
  ├─────────────┼──────────────┼──────┼──────┼──────┼──────┤
  │ Kyiv oblast │ 0.15         │ 0.37 │ 0.57 │ 0.79 │ 0.96 │
  ├─────────────┼──────────────┼──────┼──────┼──────┼──────┤
  │ Odesa       │ 0.34         │ 0.66 │ 0.86 │ 0.96 │ 1.00 │
  ├─────────────┼──────────────┼──────┼──────┼──────┼──────┤
  │ Poltava     │ 0.34         │ 0.68 │ 0.87 │ 0.98 │ 1.00 │
  ├─────────────┼──────────────┼──────┼──────┼──────┼──────┤
  │ Chernihiv   │ 0.52         │ 0.84 │ 0.94 │ 0.99 │ 1.00 │
  └─────────────┴──────────────┴──────┴──────┴──────┴──────┘

  Downsides of more horizons

  1. Long horizons stop carrying information. At 12–24 h, half the oblasts are
     at 0.96–1.00, so "yes, there will be an alert" is trivially right. A good
     score there wouldn't mean anything. Beyond about 12 h, only the west and
     centre are informative.
  2. Separate models per horizon can contradict each other. One model might say
     40% within 3 h and another 35% within 6 h, which is impossible. With many
     horizons this would happen often and look sloppy.
  3. Each horizon removes its own length from the end of the test period. A 24 h
     label at 9 Oct 18:00 needs data up to 10 Oct 18:00. A 24 h horizon costs
     one test day out of about 27; a 72 h horizon would cost three.
  4. More reporting work. Every horizon needs its metrics, calibration plot and
     comparison with the baseline. It's two days, so that time matters.

  Better design: one model that gives the whole 1–24 h curve

  Instead of N separate classifiers, predict the hourly hazard: the probability
  the alert starts in hour k (k = 1…24), given it hasn't started yet.
  Multiplying those through gives the probability for every horizon from 1 to 24
  h in one consistent, always-rising curve.

  - In practice: one LightGBM with "hours ahead" as a feature, trained on
    (oblast, t, k) rows. This is the standard discrete-time survival setup.
  - It answers the original question: we get every hourly horizon instead of
    four.
  - We report metrics at 1/3/6/12 h, show the full curve in the CLI, and
    evaluate 24 h only for the informative oblasts.

  Cost: about 24× more training rows (around 2.4M). That's still only minutes
  for LightGBM, and a bit more feature code.

  Also from this table

  - Chernihiv (73% of the time under alert in summer) should join the
    inputs-only group with Sumy, Zaporizhzhia, Kharkiv, Donetsk and Dnipro.
  - Western oblasts have so few alerts at 1 h (about 1%) that PR-AUC will be
    noisy there. I'd report them as a separate group rather than mix them into
    the average.

  My recommendation: the hazard model, horizons 1–24 h, with the headline
  numbers at 1/3/6/12 h. Shall I go with that and start the alerts.in.ua fetch?

  On merit, my recommendation is the same: one hazard model that gives a

⏺ User answered Claude's questions:
  ⎿  · Which geographic level should the model forecast? Since Dec 2025 most 
     declarations are raion-level (~130 raions), and alerts.in.ua also has raion
     data. → It's not kyiv special, let's make it merged raions oblast for now.
     I'm not sure raions make a lot of sense. 
     · Who consumes the forecast, i.e. what does the final artifact look like? →
     Obviosuly need a UI + CLI. 
     · What does a 'good forecast' mean for you? This decides the headline 
     metric. → Honest probabilities (Recommended)
     · Telegram text features: include a small ablation (kpszsu only, national 
     takeoff/launch keyword counts via Telethon, edited posts dropped, no 
     geocoding)? → Ablation as stretch (Recommended)

⏺ User answered Claude's questions:
  ⎿  · What kind of UI? → Static HTML from Python (Recommended)
     · What should the UI show? (pick all you want) → Forecast map, Replay over 
     test period, Model scorecard, Data/EDA page, "A nice way to demo is to show
     two charts for this replay thing, which is replay chart(with actual 
     predictions) vs actual. Think on a way to display it the easiest for the 
     user "
     · Frontline oblasts (Dnipro, Donetsk, Kharkiv, Sumy, Zaporizhzhia, 
     Chernihiv) are under alert 73–100% of the time, so few 'quiet' hours to 
     forecast. How to treat them? → Why cant we score them, explain to me, why 
     can't model just say that it will be most likely not quiet considering 
     entire history?   

⏺ You can score them, and the model will predict them. "Inputs only" was the
  wrong call. The real problem is something else.

  Why the frontline oblasts look odd under the hazard target:
  - The hazard model only asks "will an alert start in the next H hours?" at
    hours when the oblast is quiet. If it's already under alert, the question
    doesn't apply.
  - Frontline oblasts are almost never quiet. In the June–August official data,
    Dnipro had 1 quiet hour, Donetsk 21, Kharkiv 156, Sumy 235, and Kyiv City
    about 1,960.
  - So "nothing will start" never even comes up there. The UI simply shows them
    as "under alert now" most of the time.
  - On the rare quiet hours, the model would say an alert is very likely soon
    (Sumy: 98% within 3 h). That's correct, it's just a trivial result.

  What goes wrong if we score them together with everything else:
  1. Too few rows to measure: with 1 to 235 quiet hours, the per-oblast metric
     is noise.
  2. They inflate the average: near-certain, near-correct predictions make the
     pooled metric look better than the model really is.

  The fix:
  - Train on every oblast's quiet hours, including the frontline ones (more
    data).
  - Score everything, but report three groups separately: west, centre/south,
    frontline. The headline number excludes the frontline group.

  The deeper issue is the merged-raion definition itself. Dnipro is "100% under
  alert" because frontline raions such as Nikopolskyi are almost always under
  alert. Dnipro city itself is often quiet, and merging hides that. The same
  likely applies to Kharkiv and Zaporizhzhia. There is a middle option: drop the
  near-permanent raions from each oblast's merged timeline, using a threshold
  chosen on training data only.

  On your replay idea, my proposal for the simplest view:
  - Timeline per oblast: one oblast selector and one chart over the test month.
    The predicted P(alert within 3 h) is a line, actual alerts are red shaded
    bands underneath, and a horizon dropdown sits on top. You can read hits and
    misses straight off it: the line should rise before the bands start.
  - Side-by-side maps: a time slider drives two maps. On the left is the
    forecast made at that moment; on the right is what actually happened in the
    next H hours.

⏺ User declined to answer questions
  ⎿  · How should an oblast's alert timeline be built, given that a few 
     frontline raions are under alert almost constantly? (Drop near-permanent 
     raions (Recommended) / Plain union of all raions)
     · Replay view: which layout? (Both (Recommended) / Timeline chart only / 
     Side-by-side maps only)

✻ Baked for 55s · done Saturday 6:17 PM

❯ Dont we answer on the question? when is it going to be quite? and is it worth 
  thinking about this?                                                          

⏺ Right, so far we only answer half the question. The hazard model asks "will an
  alert start?", and that's only meaningful while an oblast is quiet. The other
  half is "this alert is running, when will it be quiet?", and we haven't
  covered it yet. For frontline oblasts, which are under alert 73–100% of the
  time, that's practically the only question that matters.

  How it works: it's the same model in reverse.
  - For an oblast under alert at t, predict P(the alert ends within (t, t+H]).
  - Same survival setup, same features, plus how long this alert has been
    running so far.
  - Together the two models give every oblast a forecast at every moment:
    - quiet: "chance an alert starts within H";
    - under alert: "chance it's over within H".
  - The map then has no greyed-out regions.

  Is it worth it? I think yes:
  - Usefulness: "when can I leave the shelter" is what people actually ask, the
    same need behind silent-sky and ballistic-kyiv.
  - Data: active hours are plentiful, especially in the frontline oblasts.
  - Cost: it reuses the pipeline. It adds one more label definition, one more
    evaluation block and one more UI state.

  What it requires:
  1. A finer time step. Median alerts last about 20–35 minutes, so with hourly
     forecast times most alerts would start and end between two forecasts. The
     ending model needs forecasts every 10–15 minutes, with horizons in
     15-minute steps up to about 6 h. The starting model can stay hourly.
  2. Alerts still running at the end of the test window are fine: the survival
     setup handles them, because it only uses the hours where the outcome is
     known.
  3. The raion question becomes unavoidable. With a plain merge of all raions,
     Dnipro is under alert 100% of the time because of Nikopol, so "when will it
     be quiet?" always answers "never". That's true for the merged definition
     and useless as a forecast. Dropping near-permanent raions (chosen on
     training data) fixes both models at once.
  4. End times may mean something different after 6 Sep. The yellow/red system
     abolished the second all-clear signal, so how alerts.in.ua records an
     alert's end in the test month may differ from the training period. I'd
     check this in the first fetch, before trusting the ending model.

  My recommendation: build both, the starting and the ending model, and drop
  near-permanent raions before merging. Do you agree? If yes, I'll write the
  full plan with both models, the replay views and the scorecard.

✻ Brewed for 20s · done Saturday 6:17 PM

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ The plan is in ~/$code/personal/kse/PLAN.md. Here's the product in summary.

  The question it answers, per oblast, at any moment:
  - Quiet: what's the chance an alert starts within the next 1–24 h?
  - Under alert: what's the chance it's over within the next 15 min – 6 h?

  Data:
  - Training: official declarations, deduplicated, Dec 2025 to Jul 2026.
    Validation is August.
  - Test: the alerts.in.ua 30-day history, fetched once. Its first 72 h are used
    only as feature history, not scored.
  - Oblast timeline: all raion alerts in the oblast merged into one timeline.
    Raions under alert more than 60% of the time in training are dropped first;
    that's why Dnipro, Kharkiv and Zaporizhzhia can be forecast at all.
  - Dropped: volunteer data and weather.

  Models:
  - Two discrete-time survival models in LightGBM, one for "will it start" and
    one for "when will it end". Each gives a whole probability curve that can
    only rise with the horizon.
  - Baselines:
    - Start: hour-of-week averages, plus logistic regression.
    - End: the historical duration curve (Kaplan–Meier), conditioned on how long
      the alert has already run, plus logistic regression.
  - Features: calendar, the oblast's own recent alerts, neighbours (a
    hand-written adjacency map), national state and recent waves.
  - Frozen after validation: no retraining.

  No leakage:
  - One feature function serves training, testing, the CLI and the UI.
  - A test checks that features computed on the full data equal features
    computed on data cut off at t.
  - No training example's outcome window crosses a split boundary.
  - The dropped-raion list and the baselines come from training data only;
    tuning and calibration use validation only.
  - The test set is scored once and logged.

  Accuracy:
  - The headline is the Brier skill score against the baseline, with day-block
    bootstrap confidence intervals, plus calibration plots.
  - Also reported: integrated Brier over the whole curve, log loss, and PR-AUC.
  - Results are split into west, centre/south and frontline; the headline
    excludes the frontline group.

  UI: a static HTML site with four tabs: forecast map, replay (probability line
  over actual-alert bands, plus forecast-vs-actual maps side by side),
  scorecard, and data. There's also a CLI alerts forecast --at.

  Stretch: a Telegram ablation using only the Air Force channel @kpszsu, with
  launch/takeoff keyword counts and no geocoding.

  The first step is still checking the test source: pull the alerts.in.ua month
  and confirm it has raion-level alerts, IDs that map onto the official names,
  and how alert ends are recorded after 6 Sep. If it fails, we test on August
  official data and say so in the README.

  I also recorded these decisions on the KSE vault page.

     │ Air Raid Alert Hazard Forecast — Plan                                   │

     │                                                                         │
     │ KSE Agentic AI School, Stage 2: a Python mini-project on time series    │
     │ analysis of Ukrainian air raid alerts. Evaluate on the latest period    │
     │ with zero leakage: nothing used for training or as input may come from  │
     │ after the forecast time. Graded mainly on process (AI log), then on the │
     │ artifact.                                                               │
     │                                                                         │
     │ The June 2026 cohort solved the same task with national daily counts +  │
     │ ADF/STL + SARIMA/ETS/Prophet + Streamlit. This project instead answers  │
     │ the question a person actually has, per oblast, at any moment:          │
     │                                                                         │
     │ - Oblast is quiet: what is the probability an alert starts within the   │
     │   next 1–24 h?                                                          │
     │ - Oblast is under alert: what is the probability it is over within the  │

     │                                                                         │

     │                                                                         │
