     ╭─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────╮
     │ Air Raid Alert Hazard Forecast — Plan                                   │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Context                                                                 │
     │                                                                         │
     │ KSE Agentic AI School, Stage 2: a Python mini-project on time series    │
     │ analysis of Ukrainian air raid alerts. Evaluate on the latest period    │
     │ with zero leakage: nothing used for training or as input may come from  │
     │ after the forecast time. Graded mainly on process (AI log), then on the │
     │ artifact.                                                               │
     │                                                                         │
     │ The June 2026 cohort solved the same task with national daily counts +  │
     │ ADF/STL + SARIMA/ETS/Prophet + Streamlit. This project instead answers  │
     │ the questions a person actually has, per oblast, at any moment:         │
     │                                                                         │
     │ - Oblast is quiet: what is the probability an alert starts within the   │
     │   next 1–24 h?                                                          │
     │ - Oblast is under alert: what is the probability it is over within the  │
     │   next 15 min – 6 h?                                                    │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Each question is asked for two levels, matching the yellow/red alert    │
     │ system introduced on 6 Sep 2026: any alert (yellow or red) and red      │
     │ alert (missile, ballistic or massed attack).                            │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Key data findings that shape the design                                 │
     │                                                                         │
     │ 1. The alert definition changed on 6 Sep 2026.                          │
     │    - Before: one siren-backed alert.                                    │
     │    - Since: yellow (drone threat) and red. 63% of air raid alerts in    │
     │      the test month are yellow.                                         │
     │    - Share of time under alert, before vs. after, any (red-only in      │
     │      brackets):                                                         │
     │                                                                         │
     │ | Region      | Before (official, Dec–Jul) | After (alerts.in.ua, 10    │
     │ Sep–10 Oct) |                                                           │
     │ |-------------|----------------------------|--------------------------- │
     │ ----------|                                                             │
     │ | Kyiv City   | 5.9%                       | 31.1% (red 2.6%)           │
     │          |                                                              │
     │ | Kyiv oblast | 17%                        | 67.7% (red 11.4%)          │
     │          |                                                              │
     │                                                                         │
     │    - Pre-September data is a different definition. It is used           │
     │      deliberately, never silently mixed in.                             │
     │ 2. Artillery is mixed into the official data.                           │
     │    - The official CSV has no type column. Its hromada-level rows are    │
     │      artillery-shelling hromadas (Nikopol, Marhanets, Pokrovsk…).       │
     │    - In alerts.in.ua, artillery_shelling appears only at hromada/city   │
     │      level, never at raion or oblast level.                             │
     │ 3. The volunteer (eTryvoga) data doesn't match the declarations:        │
     │    - only ~50% of its starts match an official start exactly, and ~30%  │
     │      have no official start within 5 min;                               │
     │    - it shows alerts in western oblasts that were never declared.       │
     │                                                                         │
     │    Not used.                                                            │
     │ 4. The official CSV has every row twice, a gap from 29 Aug to 1 Sep,    │
     │    and stops on 7 Sep, because its upstream channel went silent.        │
     │ 5. No raion is dropped. Raion coverage has no natural gap between 50%   │
     │    and 100%, and threshold cuts either wipe out whole oblasts or remove │
     │    city raions. Near-permanent oblasts are shown as they are.           │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Product                                                                 │
     │                                                                         │
     │ - CLI: alerts forecast --at "2026-10-09T18:00" prints, per oblast:      │
     │   - its state: quiet, or under yellow/red alert and for how long;       │
     │   - the "any" and "red" curves.                                         │
     │ - Static HTML site (alerts report → site/index.html, GitHub Pages, no   │
     │   server), four tabs:                                                   │
     │   a. Forecast map at the latest data time.                              │
     │      - Toggles: Any / Red, and horizon.                                 │
     │      - Colour: quiet oblasts by P(start within H); oblasts under alert  │
     │        by P(end within H), in a second colour scale.                    │
     │      - Clicking an oblast shows both curves.                            │
     │   b. Replay over the test periods, readable without explanation:        │
     │      - Timeline chart (main view): oblast selector, Any/Red toggle,     │
     │        horizon dropdown. The predicted probability is a line; actual    │
     │        alerts are yellow and red bands beneath it on the same time      │
     │        axis. A good forecast rises before a band starts.                │
     │      - Side-by-side maps: one time slider. Left map = forecast made at  │
     │        that moment; right map = what actually happened in the next H    │
     │        hours.                                                           │
     │   c. Scorecard: skill against baselines per horizon for evaluations E1  │
     │      and E2, calibration plots, per-oblast table, the old-data          │
     │      ablation, feature importance.                                      │
     │   d. Data:                                                              │
     │      - hour-of-day and day-of-week profiles, oblast co-occurrence;      │
     │      - per-raion coverage vs. the merged oblast. Finding:               │
     │        Dnipropetrovsk oblast's raw union is near-permanent, while       │
     │        Dniprovskyi raion, where Dnipro city is, is under alert only     │
     │        23%;                                                             │
     │      - regime shifts: Dec 2025 raion-level switch; the Sep 2026         │
     │        yellow/red switch with the before/after table above.             │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Data                                                                    │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Source: Vadimkin official_data_uk.csv                                   │
     │ What: Scraped @air_alert_ua declarations, old system, no type/level     │
     │ Window (UTC): 2025-12-01 → 2026-08-28                                   │
     │ ────────────────────────────────────────                                │
     │ Source: alerts.in.ua /v1/regions/{uid}/alerts/month_ago.json            │
     │ What: Declarations with alert_type, alert_level (red/yellow),           │
     │ oblast/raion/hromada/city, new system                                   │
     │ Window (UTC): ~2026-09-10 → last fetch                                  │
     │                                                                         │
     │ - alerts.in.ua is a rolling 30-day window. Each alerts fetch adds to    │
     │   data/alerts_in_ua.parquet (deduplicated by id), never replaces it, or │
     │   the early September data is lost.                                     │
     │   - The first fetch was made on 2026-10-10 (16,269 alerts, 26 regions); │
     │     its raw JSON is kept in data/raw/alerts_in_ua_2026-10-10/.          │
     │   - A final refetch on 12 Oct adds two test days.                       │
     │ - Token: ALERTS_IN_UA_TOKEN from the environment, never committed. Rate │
     │   limit 2 requests/min, so a full fetch takes ~15 min.                  │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Building the timelines                                                  │
     │                                                                         │
     │ 1. Canonical table: source, oblast, raion, location_type, alert_type,   │
     │    level, started_at, finished_at, all UTC. One adapter per source;     │
     │    official rows get level = NULL and alert_type = NULL. Duplicate rows │
     │    are removed.                                                         │
     │ 2. Same rule in both systems: only oblast- and raion-level declarations │
     │    are used.                                                            │
     │    - It removes 100% of artillery alerts in the new data and, by the    │
     │      same logic, the artillery hromadas in the old data. "Any alert" is │
     │      then defined identically before and after September.               │
     │    - Documented cost: in the new data, city-only air raid alerts are    │
     │      lost: Kirovohrad 19% of its air-raid time, Kharkiv 10%, Dnipro 4%. │
     │ 3. Two event streams per oblast:                                        │
     │    - any = union of all intervals (old alerts, or new red + yellow);    │
     │    - red = union of red intervals, new system only.                     │
     │                                                                         │
     │    Quiet gaps shorter than 2 min are bridged so overlapping raion       │
     │    alerts don't flicker.                                                │
     │ 4. Units: 24 oblasts + Kyiv City. Luhansk and Crimea are excluded:      │
     │    permanent alerts, not forecastable.                                  │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Models                                                                  │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Discrete-time hazard (survival) models. For each future time bin k, the │
     │ model predicts the probability that the event happens in bin k given    │
     │ it hasn't happened before. The cumulative probability is P(event ≤ H) = │
     │ 1 − Π_{k≤H}(1 − h_k). One model gives the whole curve, and the curve    │
     │ never goes down as H grows.                                             │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Four models from one parametrised pipeline:                             │
     │                                                                         │
     │ ┌───────────┬───────────────────────┬────────┬────────────┬──────────── │
     │ ──┐                                                                     │
     │ │           │    Forecast times     │  Bins  │   Event    │  Reported   │
     │  │                                                                      │
     │ │           │                       │        │            │   horizons  │
     │  │                                                                      │
     │ ├───────────┼───────────────────────┼────────┼────────────┼──────────── │
     │ ──┤                                                                     │
     │ │ any-start │ hourly, oblast quiet  │ 24 × 1 │ any alert  │ 1, 3, 6,    │
     │ 12, │                                                                   │
     │ │           │ (no alert) at t       │  h     │ starts     │  24 h       │
     │  │                                                                      │
     │ ├───────────┼───────────────────────┼────────┼────────────┼──────────── │
     │ ──┤                                                                     │
     │ │ any-end   │ every 15 min, oblast  │ 24 ×   │ oblast     │ 15, 30, 60, │
     │  │                                                                      │
     │ │           │ under any alert at t  │ 15 min │ goes quiet │ 120 min     │
     │  │                                                                      │
     │ ├───────────┼───────────────────────┼────────┼────────────┼──────────── │
     │ ──┤                                                                     │
     │ │ red-start │ hourly, oblast not    │ 24 × 1 │ red alert  │ 1, 3, 6,    │
     │ 12, │                                                                   │
     │ │           │ under red at t        │  h     │ starts     │  24 h       │
     │  │                                                                      │
     │ ├───────────┼───────────────────────┼────────┼────────────┼──────────── │
     │ ──┤                                                                     │
     │ │ red-end   │ every 15 min, oblast  │ 24 ×   │ red ends   │ 15, 30, 60, │
     │  │                                                                      │
     │ │           │ under red at t        │ 15 min │            │ 120 min     │
     │  │                                                                      │
     │ └───────────┴───────────────────────┴────────┴────────────┴──────────── │
     │ ──┘                                                                     │
     │                                                                         │
     │ - Training rows: each (oblast, t) expands into one row per bin k up to  │
     │   and including the bin where the event happens. Label 1 in that bin, 0 │
     │   before it.                                                            │
     │ - Censoring: if the data ends before any event, the row stops at the    │
     │   last fully observed bin.                                              │
     │ - Bin index: k is a feature.                                            │
     │                                                                         │
     │ How the old data is used                                                │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Model: any-start, any-end                                               │
     │ Training data: Old rows + new rows, with a regime feature (old/new).    │
     │ The old                                                                 │
     │ alert is treated as the closest match to "any" (one signal for any air  │
     │ threat). Red/yellow input features are empty for old rows; LightGBM     │
     │ handles                                                                 │
     │ missing values natively.                                                │
     │ ────────────────────────────────────────                                │
     │ Model: red-start, red-end                                               │
     │ Training data: New rows only (old rows have no red label). The          │
     │ any-model's                                                             │
     │ forecast at the same t is a feature, so red benefits from the old data  │
     │ indirectly.                                                             │
     │                                                                         │
     │ LightGBM setup                                                          │
     │                                                                         │
     │ - objective=binary, no class weights, so probabilities stay honest.     │
     │ - learning_rate=0.05, early stopping on validation log loss.            │
     │ - Small grid on validation only, about 8 configurations: num_leaves ∈   │
     │   {15, 31, 63}, min_data_in_leaf ∈ {200, 1000}, feature_fraction 0.8.   │
     │ - oblast as a categorical feature: one pooled model across oblasts.     │
     │ - Calibration: check reliability on validation. If needed, apply        │
     │   temperature scaling: a single scalar applied to the hazard logits,    │
     │   fitted on validation. It keeps the curve rising with H.               │
     │ - Consistency: the displayed P(red ≤ H) is capped at P(any ≤ H), and    │
     │   the report counts how often the cap is applied.                       │
     │ - Frozen after validation. No retraining before the test.               │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Baselines (each must be beaten)                                         │
     │                                                                         │
     │ - Start models:                                                         │
     │   - Climatology: per oblast, hour of week and bin k, the empirical      │
     │     hazard over the training window.                                    │
     │   - Logistic regression on the same rows and features.                  │
     │ - End models:                                                           │
     │   - Kaplan–Meier per oblast, conditioned on elapsed duration ("alerts   │
     │     that have lasted 40 min so far end within 15 min X% of the time").  │
     │   - Logistic regression.                                                │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Features                                                                │
     │                                                                         │
     │ One function features(events, t) serves training, validation, test, the │
     │ CLI and the UI. An alert is visible at t only if started_at ≤ t. Its    │
     │ end is visible only if finished_at ≤ t; otherwise it counts as still    │
     │ active at t.                                                            │
     │                                                                         │
     │ - Calendar: hour of day and day of week in Kyiv time. No date or trend  │
     │   index.                                                                │
     │ - Regime: old/new.                                                      │
     │ - Own oblast:                                                           │
     │   - current state duration: minutes since the last end, or minutes      │
     │     elapsed in the current alert;                                       │
     │   - current level (none/yellow/red; empty for old rows);                │
     │   - decayed start counts with half-lives 3 h, 24 h, 72 h, separately    │
     │     for any and red;                                                    │
     │   - number of starts in the last 24 h;                                  │
     │   - share of raions active now.                                         │
     │ - Neighbours (hand-written adjacency map of 25 units):                  │
     │   - number under any alert and under red now;                           │
     │   - minutes since the last neighbour start;                             │
     │   - neighbour starts in the last 30, 60 and 120 min.                    │
     │ - National:                                                             │
     │   - number of oblasts under any alert and under red now;                │
     │   - starts nationwide in the last 60 min;                               │
     │   - hours since the last national wave (≥15 oblasts starting within 30  │
     │     min);                                                               │
     │   - state of each other oblast, so the model can learn routes (Sumy →   │
     │     Poltava → Kyiv).                                                    │
     │ - Red models only: the any-model's P(≤ H) at t.                         │
     │ - Structure: oblast (categorical) and bin index k.                      │
     │ - Lookback: at most 72 h, so the first 72 h after any data gap are      │
     │   warm-up and are not scored.                                           │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Evaluation                                                              │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Two evaluations, because the definition changed:                        │
     │                                                                         │
     │ ┌────────────┬──────────────────────────────────┬────────────────────── │
     │ ───┐                                                                    │
     │ │            │                                  │ E2: latest period,    │
     │ new  │                                                                  │
     │ │            │   E1: method check, old system   │   system (the task's  │
     │   │                                                                     │
     │ │            │                                  │      requirement)     │
     │   │                                                                     │
     │ ├────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────── │
     │ ───┤                                                                    │
     │ │            │                                  │ old data → 2026-08-28 │
     │ + │                                                                     │
     │ │ Train      │ official 2025-12-01 → 2026-06-29 │  new data 2026-09-13  │
     │ →  │                                                                    │
     │ │            │                                  │ 09-27                 │
     │   │                                                                     │
     │ ├────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────── │
     │ ───┤                                                                    │
     │ │ Validation │ official 2026-07-01 → 07-28      │ new data 2026-09-28 → │
     │   │                                                                     │
     │ │            │                                  │ 10-01                 │
     │   │                                                                     │
     │ ├────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────── │
     │ ───┤                                                                    │
     │ │ Test       │ official 2026-07-30 → 08-28      │ new data 2026-10-02 → │
     │   │                                                                     │
     │ │            │                                  │ last fetch (~10–12    │
     │ Oct) │                                                                  │
     │ ├────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────── │
     │ ───┤                                                                    │
     │ │ Models     │ any-start, any-end               │ all four              │
     │   │                                                                     │
     │ ├────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┼────────────────────── │
     │ ───┤                                                                    │
     │ │            │ Stable definition and ~4 weeks × │ Current system; small │
     │   │                                                                     │
     │ │ Why        │  25 oblasts of test data: tight  │ test, so wide         │
     │   │                                                                     │
     │ │            │ confidence intervals; answers    │ confidence intervals, │
     │   │                                                                     │
     │ │            │ "does the method work at all"    │ stated openly         │
     │   │                                                                     │
     │ └────────────┴──────────────────────────────────┴────────────────────── │
     │ ───┘                                                                    │
     │                                                                         │
     │ - Split boundaries: each is separated by at least 24 h. An example at   │
     │   time t is kept only if t + its longest horizon ≤ the end of its       │
     │   split.                                                                │
     │ - Old-data ablation on E2 (any-start and any-end):                      │
     │   - (a) trained on new data only;                                       │
     │   - (b) old + new with the regime feature (the main model);             │
     │   - (c) old data only, applied as is.                                   │
     │                                                                         │
     │   If (b) beats (a), old data has been shown to help. The gap between    │
     │   (c) and the others puts the regime shift into numbers.                │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Measuring accuracy                                                      │
     │                                                                         │
     │ The headline is probability quality: when the model says 30%, the event │
     │ happens about 30% of the time.                                          │
     │                                                                         │
     │ - Brier score at each reported horizon: mean of (p − outcome)².         │
     │ - Brier skill score: 1 − Brier_model / Brier_baseline against           │
     │   climatology (start models) or Kaplan–Meier (end models). This is the  │
     │   headline number: >0 means the model beats history alone.              │
     │ - Integrated Brier score over the whole curve, as one number per model. │
     │ - Log loss at each horizon.                                             │
     │ - PR-AUC for start models: events are rare in the west, so ROC-AUC      │
     │   would flatter.                                                        │
     │ - Calibration: reliability diagram (10 bins) plus expected calibration  │
     │   error per horizon.                                                    │
     │ - Uncertainty: 95% confidence intervals for the skill score from a      │
     │   day-block bootstrap.                                                  │
     │ - Breakdown:                                                            │
     │   - three oblast groups: west, centre/south, frontline. The start-model │
     │     headline excludes the frontline group, which has few quiet hours;   │
     │     end models are reported in full. Near-permanent oblasts are marked  │
     │     as such, with their trivial forecasts shown as is;                  │
     │   - per-oblast table;                                                   │
     │   - by hour of day.                                                     │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Leakage controls                                                        │
     │                                                                         │
     │ 1. One feature code path: features(events, t) filters the events        │
     │    itself.                                                              │
     │ 2. Property test (tests/test_leakage.py): for ~200 random t in both     │
     │    regimes, features computed on the full data equal features computed  │
     │    on data truncated at t, where alerts that start after t are removed  │
     │    and end times after t are blanked.                                   │
     │ 3. No label crosses a split boundary: examples whose horizon window     │
     │    crosses the end of their split are dropped; splits are separated by  │
     │    at least 24 h.                                                       │
     │ 4. Fixed in advance: the adjacency map and the level rule are written   │
     │    by hand; climatology and Kaplan–Meier come from training data;       │
     │    tuning and calibration use validation only.                          │
     │ 5. Test touched once per evaluation: alerts evaluate --eval e1|e2       │
     │    --split test writes reports/<eval>_test_metrics.json with a          │
     │    timestamp and the frozen model hash, both logged in the README.      │
     │ 6. Red models: they read the any-model's forecast at the same t, which  │
     │    is itself built only from data up to t.                              │
     │ 7. Telegram (stretch only): messages used by message.date ≤ t; edited   │
     │    messages are dropped.                                                │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Telegram ablation (stretch)                                             │
     │                                                                         │
     │ - Source: @kpszsu only, through Telethon using the local telegram skill │
     │   session (read-only, no channel joins), adapting                       │
     │   ~/$code/PET/ballistic-kyiv/backend/scripts/pull_corpus.py.            │
     │ - Features: national counts in the last 30/60/120 min of takeoff/launch │
     │   posts (MiG-31K, Tu-95/160, ballistic, launch), reusing the regex      │
     │   approach of ballistic-kyiv/backend/classifier.py. No geocoding.       │
     │ - Reported as: E2 red-start and any-start skill with vs. without these  │
     │   features.                                                             │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Repo                                                                    │
     │                                                                         │
     │ kse/                                                                    │
     │   pyproject.toml          uv, Python 3.12: pandas, numpy, pyarrow,      │
     │ lightgbm, scikit-learn, httpx, plotly, typer, pytest                    │
     │   src/alerts/                                                           │
     │     sources.py            official CSV + alerts.in.ua (append-only      │
     │ store) -> canonical table                                               │
     │     timeline.py           level rule, any/red streams, forecast times,  │
     │ hazard row expansion                                                    │
     │     adjacency.py          oblast neighbour map                          │
     │     features.py           features(events, t)                           │
     │     models.py             climatology, Kaplan-Meier, logistic           │
     │ regression, LightGBM, temperature scaling                               │
     │     evaluate.py           E1/E2 splits, metrics, bootstrap,             │
     │ reliability, ablation                                                   │
     │     site.py               static HTML (Plotly + oblast GeoJSON)         │
     │     cli.py                fetch | train | evaluate | forecast | report  │
     │   tests/test_leakage.py                                                 │
     │   reports/                generated metrics + plots                     │
     │   site/                   generated HTML for GitHub Pages               │
     │   data/, artifacts/       gitignored; data/raw keeps every alerts.in.ua │
     │ fetch (append-only store, back it up)                                   │
     │                                                                         │
     │ - Reuse:                                                                │
     │   - alerts.in.ua field names follow                                     │
     │     ~/$code/PET/silent-sky/backend/services/alerts_in_ua.py;            │
     │   - oblast boundaries come from the OCHA admin1 GeoJSON in silent-sky   │
     │     backend/data/.cache.                                                │
     │ - Region UIDs: verified on 2026-10-10. 3 Khmelnytska, 4 Vinnytska, 5    │
     │   Rivnenska, 8 Volynska, 9 Dnipropetrovska, 10 Zhytomyrska, 11          │
     │   Zakarpatska, 12 Zaporizka, 13 Ivano-Frankivska, 14 Kyivska, 15        │
     │   Kirovohradska, 16 Luhanska, 17 Mykolaivska, 18 Odeska, 19 Poltavska,  │
     │   20 Sumska, 21 Ternopilska, 22 Kharkivska, 23 Khersonska, 24           │
     │   Cherkaska, 25 Chernihivska, 26 Chernivetska, 27 Lvivska, 28 Donetska, │
     │   29 Crimea, 31 Kyiv City.                                              │
     │ - Code style: no code comments, per Bohdan's conventions.               │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Submission                                                              │
     │                                                                         │
     │ - GitHub repo with a README covering:                                   │
     │   - the questions answered;                                             │
     │   - data provenance (why the volunteer data was rejected, the artillery │
     │     rule, the regime shift);                                            │
     │   - E1 and E2 protocol and results with confidence intervals;           │
     │   - the old-data ablation;                                              │
     │   - the site link;                                                      │
     │   - known limits.                                                       │
     │ - AI log: full export of this Claude Code session.                      │
     │ - Reflection: ~100 words, plain text. What went wrong:                  │
     │   - the first plan was built on the volunteer data;                     │
     │   - the official CSV turned out to be duplicated, stalled and mixed     │
     │     with artillery alerts;                                              │
     │   - the yellow/red switch made "alert" a different event.               │
     │                                                                         │
     │   How the approach changed, and why the final version beats the first   │
     │   attempt.                                                              │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Verification                                                            │
     │                                                                         │
     │ - uv run pytest: the leakage property test passes in both regimes.      │
     │ - uv run alerts fetch && uv run alerts train && uv run alerts evaluate  │
     │   --eval e1 --split val and --eval e2 --split val: models beat their    │
     │   baselines on validation before any test set is touched.               │
     │ - uv run alerts evaluate --eval e1 --split test, then --eval e2 --split │
     │   test, each run once.                                                  │
     │ - uv run alerts forecast --at <time in E2 test>: compare by hand with   │
     │   the replay at the same time.                                          │
     │ - uv run alerts report: open site/index.html, check all tabs; check the │
     │   replay against known events (e.g. the 13–14 Sep mass strike,          │
     │   late-September Kyiv days with 12–18 h of alerts).                     │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Risks                                                                   │
     │                                                                         │
     │ - The E2 test is about 8–10 days: wide confidence intervals. That's why │
     │   E1 exists; both are reported.                                         │
     │ - Weekly recalibration of yellow/red criteria by the General Staff adds │
     │   non-stationarity within the new system. It is reported, not           │
     │   modelled.                                                             │
     │ - Red events are rare per oblast: red models are pooled, and western    │
     │   oblasts are reported as a group.                                      │
     │ - The alerts.in.ua rolling window: a fetch that replaced the store      │
     │   instead of adding to it would lose data, so the store is append-only  │
     │   and every raw fetch is kept in data/raw/.                             │
     ╰─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────╯

  ## Verdict
  The numbers are computed correctly. My own code reproduces every Brier skill
  figure, every event rate and every confidence interval in README.md and in the
  reports, exactly. The baselines are fitted only on training data, with no
  peeking, and the model and baseline are always scored on the same rows. The
  one real fairness issue is with the E2 validation numbers. That same
  validation data was used to tune the model (early stopping, grid search,
  temperature scaling) and to pick the champion. The baseline got no tuning, so
  those E2 figures favour the model. E1 test is the clean result: it was
  evaluated once, and `test_log.jsonl` has a single entry.

  ## 1. Recomputed metrics (own code, frontline excluded for start models,
  censoring rule `event_bin<=h | observed_bins>=h`)
  | Claim | Recomputed | Match? |
  |---|---|---|
  | E1 test any-start 9.9 / 14.7 / 12.2 / 6.8 / 2.0, CIs | identical; n =
  11346…11158 | yes |
  | E1 test any-end 2.5 / 2.1 / 3.1 / 5.8, CIs | identical | yes |
  | E2 val any-start (main / new-only / old-only), all 15 cells | identical |
  yes |
  | E2 val any-end, red-start, red-end, all cells | identical | yes |
  | E1 val, and all n / event_rate values in the json files | identical | yes |
  | `event_bin` in the parquets vs the raw timeline | 100% agree | yes |
  | "Old + new beats either alone at every horizon" | true for any-start and
  any-end | yes, but no CI behind it |

  **Censoring.** The rule keeps late rows only if an event happened, which
  pushes the measured event rate up. Re-scoring on rows that were fully observed
  for the whole horizon barely changes anything: any-start at 24 h goes from
  +15.9% to +16.2%. Not a material problem.

  ## 2. Is the comparison fair?
  - **No peeking:** the baseline is fitted on training rows of the evaluated
  regime only (`evaluate.py:113-114`). Labels are censored at each split
  boundary.
  - **Strength of the baseline:** at E2 it is a bit weak. With about 14 days of
  new-system data, the per-hour cells are mostly pulled back to a plain
  per-oblast rate.
  - **Stronger baselines I computed** (all strictly causal, using only outcomes
  fully resolved before each forecast time), as skill vs the repo baseline on E2
  val any-start:

  | Horizon | Model | Per-oblast rate, all new history | Per-oblast rate, last 7
  days | Hour-specific rate | Rate from the last 72 h |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | 1 h | +7.0% | +2.3% | +4.6% | −1.4% | +3.2% |
  | 12 h | +12.4% | +2.7% | −6.6% | −2.3% | −14% |
  | 24 h | +15.9% | −2.7% | −26% | −3.9% | −92% |

    The model beats every one of them at every horizon.
  - **red-start:** the simple per-oblast rate beats the model at 1, 3 and 6 h
  (+1.3 / +0.1 / −0.1 vs −0.4 / −3.1 / −0.8). This agrees with the README's own
  limit: red models are no better than climatology.
  - **End models:** I did not build a stronger alternative baseline for these.

  ## 3. Are the probabilities sane?
  - All curves stay between 0 and 1 and never decrease, for every model in every
  parquet.
  - Calibration is fair. Where it is off:
    - **any-start:** overconfident at the top. E2 forecasts around 64% come true
  52% of the time (86 forecasts), which is the README's stated limit.
    - **red-start at 24 h:** badly miscalibrated. Forecasts of 0.3–0.5 come true
  65–75% of the time; forecasts around 0.95 come true 74% of the time.
  - **Frontline any-start:** E2 has only 33 at-risk rows. The model averages
  0.73 within 1 h against an observed 0.94, and loses to the baseline there. On
  E1 test (466 rows) it roughly ties.

  ## 4. Share of time under alert
  | | README | Recomputed | Website shows |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | Old system, Kyiv City / Kyiv oblast / Odesa | 5.7 / 18.6 / 18.9 | 5.7 / 18.6
  / 18.9 (window Dec 1 – Jul 29) | 6.3 / 18.2 / 20.7 |
  | New any | 31.3 / 69.1 / 40.4 | 31.6 / 69.1 / 40.5 | same as recomputed |
  | New red | 2.7 / 11.9 / 6.8 | 2.7 / 11.6 / 6.6 | same as recomputed |

  Dnipropetrovsk at 88.6% also matches the Dec–Jul window. The README's
  new-system values look like they came from an earlier data fetch; they drift
  by up to 0.3 points.

  ## 5. Replay on the website
  I checked 12 random cells across all four kinds. In every one, the probability
  on the site equals the parquet value, and the shown outcome equals what
  actually happened in the timeline.

  ## 6. Bootstrap
  - E2 validation has only 5 Kyiv-day blocks, and the 5th covers just 3 hours.
  - The CIs are honestly wide:

  | E2 model | Horizon | 95% CI |
  |---|---|---|
  | any-start | 1 h | [+2, +13] |
  | any-start | 6 h | [−6, +21] |
  | any-end | 15 min | [−1.4, +1.5] |
  | red-start | 24 h | [−69, +32] |

  - With 5 blocks a percentile bootstrap is crude and tends to understate
  uncertainty.
  - E1's roughly 30 days give meaningful CIs. For the 24 h horizon they are
  slightly too narrow, because neighbouring days' outcomes overlap.

› Message from general-purpose
  ## Telegram for the alert forecast: data, findings and the no-downgrade
  protocol

  **Data.** I pulled message history read-only for 6 Sep–1 Oct from @kpszsu
  (5,490 posts) and @war_monitor (3,280 posts). I did not join, send or react.
  No other process was using the skill's session: lsof showed nothing, and the
  groceries bot uses the Bot API, not Telethon. The posts and scripts are in
  `/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-starosivets-bohdan--code-personal-kse/8a89f49e
  -c93a-41c3-9cdb-588e0650e33b/scratchpad/telegram/` and
  `.../scratchpad/scripts/` (`lead2.py`, `end.py`, `val.py`). Alert data came
  from `timeline.load` for 10 Sep–1 Oct; nothing from 2 Oct onward was touched.
  I edited nothing in kse or the other repos, and ran no train, fetch or test
  evaluation.

  ### 1. Lead time on red starts
  These numbers cover only the 18 non-frontline oblasts. Frontline oblasts have
  red on almost all the time.
  - 235 red starts fall into 58 waves (a new wave starts after a gap of more
  than 90 minutes). 45 of those waves start from quiet.
  - **Posts never come before the first red start of a wave.** All 24 of the 24
  hours with an early @kpszsu post (ballistic threat, MiG-31K takeoff, launches,
  strategic aviation) already had a red start somewhere in the previous 60
  minutes.
  - In the 5 episodes where @kpszsu posted while non-frontline red was quiet,
  red followed with a median wait of 1 minute. The post and the declaration are
  simultaneous. It is not a forecast.
  - Waves that start from quiet and had an early post in the 120 minutes before:
  9%. Random times give 8%. @war_monitor gives 4% against 8% random. That is no
  lead at all.
  - Broader @kpszsu "any missile" posts (adding in-flight ballistic, fast-target
  and cruise-missile posts): 14 of the 20 episodes that began while
  non-frontline red was quiet were false alarms. The median wait for red was 4.3
  h.
  - **One residual signal.** In hours that already had a red start somewhere, a
  post raised the chance that another non-frontline oblast turns red within 60
  minutes:
    - @kpszsu: 7.4% against 2.9% (2.6×, 25 events);
    - @war_monitor: 12.9% against 2.5% (5.1×, 40 events).

    In plain terms, the post tells a missile wave that is still spreading apart
  from routine frontline red. The model may already capture part of this through
  its national, neighbour and decay red features. Only the ablation can tell.
  - Red-end: 15 "all-clear" posts (відбій) land with about 6.3 red ends within
  ±5 minutes each, against 0.19 at random times. Again simultaneous, not
  leading.
  - **Leakage.**
    - Every message has an `edit_date`.
    - Edited more than 5 minutes after posting: 2 of the 28 early @kpszsu posts,
  13 of the 99 missile posts, 16 of the 139 @war_monitor missile posts. One
  threat post was edited 32 h later.
    - Backfilled history shows only the latest text, and deleted posts are gone,
  so it is not what was visible live.
    - A safe feature would use `message.date`, short fixed phrases only, and
  drop posts edited more than 5 minutes later. In production it would need a
  live logger that records the first text it sees.

  ### 2. Expected gain per model
  - **any-start, any-end:** none. These are drone/yellow alerts, and the posts
  arrive with or after the alert. Skip.
  - **red-end:** marginal at best (a "threat still open" flag). Skip.
  - **red-start:** small. It could help with spread inside a wave, not with
  predicting a wave before it starts.
  - **The test cannot measure it on validation.** Validation has 35
  non-frontline red starts. 13 have an early post before them, but they fall on
  about 3 attack nights, so they are really 3 independent events. A 4-day
  bootstrap cannot show a difference.

  ### 3. No-downgrade protocol
  **What the scores mean, in plain words:**
  - **Brier score** is the average squared miss. Say "30%" and nothing happens:
  the miss is 0.09. Say "30%" and it happens: 0.49. Lower is better.
  - **Brier skill** is 1 − model Brier / history-only Brier. +10% means 10%
  smaller error than history alone.
  - **Log loss** punishes being confidently wrong far harder. Saying "1%" when
  the event happens costs 4.6; saying "50%" costs 0.69.
  - **Calibration**: of all the times we said 30%, did the event happen about
  30% of the time?

  **Protocol:**
  1. Train two models the same way: the baseline and the baseline plus Telegram
  features. Keep the same rows, seeds and hyperparameters, frozen from the
  current champions, so validation-based tuning noise does not decide the
  result.
  2. Compare them on the same rows only. Use rolling-origin folds over 13 Sep–1
  Oct (for example, train up to day d and score days d+1 to d+3) to get about 15
  days to compare. Never use data from 2 Oct onward.
  3. For every model and horizon, compute ΔBrier, Δlog-loss and Δskill. Get a
  95% interval by resampling whole days from the per-day paired differences.
  4. **Adopt only if both hold:**
     - red-start at 1/3/6 h improves Brier, with the interval excluding 0;
     - no other model or horizon gets worse by more than 0.5 skill points, and
  calibration error gets no worse than +0.01.

     Otherwise reject.
  5. Automate it as `alerts ablate --features telegram`, which writes
  `reports/e2/ablation_telegram.json`. A pytest then asserts the rule on that
  file, plus a leakage test: posts after time t or edited after t must change no
  feature at t.

  ### 4. Effort and recommendation
  About 2–3 days: a feature builder with edit filtering, the ablation CLI and
  test, and the rolling folds. A live first-seen logger would be needed for
  production.

  **Recommendation: skip, and keep "Telegram not used" in the README, now backed
  by these numbers.** If anything, it is a red-start-only experiment after 4 or
  more more weeks of red data. On today's data, the test cannot show a gain
  even if one exists.

  **Not done:** I did not write anything to the vault. It is worth filing on the
  kse page: "kpszsu/war_monitor posts never lead the first red start of a wave
  (24 of 24 coincide), only spread inside a wave."
  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ Agent "Assess Telegram integration value" finished · 5m 17s

⏺ The Telegram agent finished. Its answer: Telegram would not improve the
  current model, so it is not worth adding.

  What it measured:
  - Data: it pulled @kpszsu (5,490 posts) and @war_monitor (3,280 posts) for 6
    Sep – 1 Oct. It only read; it joined nothing and sent nothing.
  - Red alerts (missiles): posts never came before the first red alert of a
    wave. In 24 of 24 cases, red was already declared somewhere in the hour
    before the post. When a post appeared while things were quiet, red followed
    about 1 minute later. The post and the alert arrive together, so the post
    can't forecast it.
  - Any alerts (drones): the posts come at the same time as the alert or after
    it. There is nothing to gain for "any" starts or ends.
  - The only real signal: during a missile wave that has already started, a post
    means more oblasts will likely turn red in the next hour (2.6–5× more
    likely). Our model already partly sees this through its "how many neighbours
    and how many oblasts are under alert now" features.
  - A leakage trap: posts get edited, one of them 32 hours later, and some get
    deleted. Old channel history is therefore not what people saw live.

  How "not worse" would be measured, if you ever try it:
  1. Train the current model twice, with and without Telegram, keeping
     everything else identical.
  2. Score both on the same hours and compare the error: the Brier score,
     roughly the average squared distance between the forecast % and what
     happened.
  3. Adopt it only if red-start forecasts get clearly better and no other
     forecast gets worse by more than half a point of skill.

  With only 4 validation days, a gain this small can't be told apart from luck.
  You'd need about 4 more weeks of red-alert data first.

  I've put these numbers into the README's Limits section, so "Telegram not
  used" is now backed by evidence instead of a hunch. The leakage audit is still
  running; I'll commit everything once it reports.

  ## Leakage audit: alerts project (no repo files edited, no test or train runs)

  `uv run pytest -q` passes (3 tests). I found one real leak, and it affects
  only the red models. The any-models and the split mechanics are clean apart
  from small evaluation biases.

  | Check | Verdict | Evidence |
  |---|---|---|
  | Minute boundaries: ceil(start/end) against `truncate` (`started <= t`,
  `finished > t` blanked) | OK | timeline.py:51-70. An alert starting exactly at
  t is active at t and is visible at cutoff t. |
  | `_since`, `_window`, `_decay` (lfilter per segment), `_onsets`, `in_wave`,
  national and neighbour features, hour/dow, `regime` | OK | features.py:40-161.
  All are causal accumulations, reset at segment starts. My probe built 40
  extra random NEW-regime cutoffs from full data and from data truncated at t: 0
  differences. |
  | `raion_share` denominator | RISK (latent) | timeline.py:94 averages over
  every raion the source ever contains, including future ones. 11 alerts.in.ua
  raions in Lviv, Ivano-Frankivsk and Zakarpattia first appear on 15 Sep 16:44,
  after training origins start (13 Sep). I checked all 4,680 rows before then:
  no value differs today, because no partial raion alerts happened there. A new
  raion in a later fetch would make it leak. |
  | any-models free of red-derived inputs | OK | evaluate.py:39-45 drops every
  `red_*` column plus `level_now`, `nb_red_now` and `nat_red_now`. The any state
  does not depend on `alert_level`. |
  | Red-level escalation | **ISSUE: confirmed leak (red models)** | See the
  reproduction below. |
  | Dedupe and coverage | OK / RISK | sources.py:79 keeps the last fetch's row,
  so later fetches overwrite `alert_level` (the same leak); later finish times
  are handled by `truncate`. sources.py:93-95: if two fetches are more than 30
  days apart, the hole between them is not marked unknown (not the case today).
  |
  | Labels censored at split end | OK, with a 1-minute peek | features.py:174:
  the end target at minute e needs `quiet[e+1]`, so a label can read the first
  minute after the split or before an unknown gap. That gap minute counts as
  quiet, so it is biased toward "ended". Negligible. |
  | Train/val/test boundaries, warmup | OK | evaluate.py:21-36 censors at
  `stop`, and the 72 h warmup requires fully known history. |
  | Baselines, scaler, logistic fitted on train only; grid, early stopping,
  temperature, champion on validation only | OK | evaluate.py:92-102,
  models.py:72-105, 191-195. Nothing is fitted on test, and the E1 test was run
  once (test_log.jsonl). |
  | How censored rows are scored | RISK (bias) | features.py:206: a row whose
  horizon is cut off at the split end still counts if its event happened. In E2
  validation, any-start at 24 h: 212 of 1,145 scored rows (18.5%) are kept only
  because the event happened. That raises the event rate from 0.771 to 0.813;
  skill moves by under 2 points. |
  | "Now" forecast | OK | site.py:127-128 calls `forecast()` on the last known
  minute only. An alert that started after that minute is not active yet, and
  one that ended after it still counts as active. |
  | Replay | Optimism (not a leak) | Explained in point 2 below. |

  ## Confirmed leak: red alerts escalated in place (repro: scratchpad/probe3.py)

  The live poll (`data/raw/active_poll.jsonl`, 985 polls) shows that escalation
  happens in place:
  - 41 of the 254 alert IDs that were ever red started yellow and turned red
  under the same ID with the same `started_at`. Example: id 275565, Sumy raion,
  yellow at 16:21, red at 16:54.
  - The yellow phase before red lasted a median of 38 min, with a maximum of 232
  min.
  - One alert went from red back to yellow in place.
  - In the history file there are no back-to-back yellow→red pairs at the same
  place, so escalation never shows up as a new alert.

  The history therefore paints red from the yellow start (timeline.py:99). For
  the red models this means:
  - During the yellow phase, `red_now`, `red_since_*`, `nb_red_now`,
  `nat_red_now` and `red_jj` encode an escalation that had not happened yet.
  - red-start labels fire too early.
  - red-end at-risk rows include time that was really yellow.

  The red models' training, validation scores and replay are all affected. At
  "Now", the inputs are honest but the models were learned from leaked history.
  The README and site claim that "every forecast uses only alerts declared
  before…" is false for the red level.

  ## What is optimism rather than leakage

  1. The E2 validation results in README:113-120 come from models early-stopped,
  grid-picked, champion-picked and temperature-scaled on those same 4 days
  (temperatures 0.88–1.17). That is selection from about 7 configurations plus
  one fitted parameter. The bias is modest, but 4 correlated days is a small
  sample. The models are not refit on validation data, so the test stays clean.
  2. The site labels the replay as "the validation days", which is honest.
  Neither the site nor the README says those days were used for tuning; one
  caveat line is needed in each.
  3. The ablation conclusion ("old data helps") also rests on these tuned
  validation numbers, but every variant was tuned the same way, so the
  comparison itself is fair.

  ## Recommended fixes, by importance

  1. **Red models:** you cannot recover the true escalation time from history.
  Either:
     - build red state only from the poller (poll period only), or
     - redefine red as "an alert that is or becomes red", feed only the levels
  of alerts finished by t, and drop the red-now features for alerts still
  active.

     Until then, label the red results as contaminated in the README and the
  site, and remove "a live poller checks…": the answer is now yes.
  2. **Scoring:** count only rows whose full horizon is observed (`observed_bins
  >= h`). This applies to `horizon_outcome` and `site._outcome`.
  3. **README and site:** say the E2 validation numbers and the replay are on
  the tuning set.
  4. **`raion_share`:** take the denominator only from raions seen up to t, or
  from a fixed raion list, and extend the leakage test to the full origin grid.
  5. **End labels:** keep the one-minute peek inside `censor`, and treat unknown
  minutes as not-quiet.

  Probe scripts are in `/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-starosivets-bohdan--code-
  personal-kse/8a89f49e-c93a-41c3-9cdb-588e0650e33b/scratchpad/` (`probe1.py` to
  `probe4.py`).

› Message from general-purpose
  **Verdict.** No official API or open dataset I could verify keeps historical
  yellow-to-red escalation times. One usable source does exist: the volunteer
  eTryvoga Telegram channel **@UkraineAlarmSignal**. It posts a timestamped
  message for each raion level change, and those messages go back to at least 7
  Sep 2026. Confidence: high that no official history source exists, high that
  eTryvoga works as a substitute (with caveats below).

  **1. alerts.in.ua: no history of level changes (verified from the docs).**
  - https://devs.alerts.in.ua documents `alert_level` (red/yellow) on active
  alerts.
  - It also documents a new `threats[]` array. Each threat has `threat_type`,
  `level` and `started_at` ("Час, з якого ця загроза відкрита"). That means live
  `active.json` already gives the time red started (the `started_at` of the red
  threat), but only while the alert is active.
  - The history example for `/v1/regions/{uid}/alerts/month_ago.json` shows no
  `alert_level` and no `threats`. The IoT endpoints only return A/P/N letters
  and keep no history.
  - No webhooks, events log or changelog are documented. The PHP SDK added
  `threats` on 7–8 Sep (Fyennyi/alerts-in-ua-php PR #35). The official Python
  SDK (alerts-ua/alerts-in-ua-py) has not been pushed since Aug 2025.
  - **Unknown, worth checking with your token:** whether real `month_ago.json`
  records include `threats[]`. The docs say `threats` lists only currently open
  threats, so closed ones are probably dropped.
  - **Unknown:** the alerts.in.ua map script (`assets/scripts/08634fab.js`)
  references internal endpoints `v4/alerts/day/:id.json`,
  `v4/regions/:id/alerts/recent.json` and `v4/alert_events/recent.json`. I
  couldn't find their host: `api.alerts.in.ua/v4/...` returned 404/405.

  **2. ukrainealarm.com: live only, no level history (partly verified).**
  - The swagger is behind Cloudflare (403 from curl), so I read it through
  client code instead.
  - bpashkovskyi/UkraineAlarm.Sdk (commit on 7 Sep, "Add yellow/red
  ActiveAlertLevels"): `GET /api/v3/alerts` returns
  `activeAlertLevels[{alertLevel, reason, createdAt}]`. `createdAt` is "Moment
  when this level became active".
  - The webhook body carries `alertLevel`, `createdAt` and `activeAlertLevels`
  (nazar-andriievych/Vidbiy `docs/ukrainealarm-api.md`).
  - `/api/v3/alerts/regionHistory` returns only the last 25 alarms (start, end,
  type, no level), and that SDK commit did not change the history model. This
  comes from an unofficial SDK, so treat it as likely rather than proven.

  **3. Vadimkin/ukrainian-air-raid-sirens-dataset: no levels.**
  - Its official feed parses the Telegram channel @air_alert_ua and stopped on 7
  Sep 2026 (issues #6 and #7).
  - I verified that @air_alert_ua's last post is #338380 at 2026-09-07 06:09
  UTC; later post ids return "Post not found".
  - The volunteer CSV, built from eTryvoga, is still updating daily but has no
  level column.

  **4. eTryvoga @UkraineAlarmSignal: yes (verified).**
  - It posts "🟡 X район / Жовтий рівень тривоги", "🔴 … Червоний рівень
  тривоги" and "🟢 Відбій", sometimes as multi-raion batches.
  - Earliest level post I saw: #614981, 2026-09-07 05:11 UTC. Messages before
  about #612000 (4 Sep) have no levels.
  - An in-place escalation is visible: Харківський район went yellow at 14:56:20
  UTC (#652750) and red at 15:16:06 (#652774) on 11 Oct, with no all-clear in
  between (all-clear at 15:28:35).
  - Caveats:
    - It is volunteer-run, not official.
    - On 7 Sep, timestamps were rounded to 10 seconds.
    - Some sequences are an all-clear followed by a new alert rather than a
  clean switch.
    - The coverage is raion and city level.
  - Full history is reachable with Telethon `iter_messages` from about id
  614000, which your existing Telegram skill can do. The public
  `t.me/s/UkraineAlarmSignal?before=` pages also work, 20 messages per page.

  **5. Official sources: no.**
  - Ajax blog (11 Sep): app version 7.2.0 shows the levels "in real time". It
  says nothing about history, an API or open data.
  - data.gov.ua was in maintenance mode, so its catalog is unknown. A web search
  found no alert-level dataset there.

  **6. Map and stats sites: no.**
  - tryvogy.com has no levels and no export.
  - GitHub searches for "alert_level", "activeAlertLevels" and "Жовтий рівень
  тривоги" turned up only live consumers: the Home Assistant integration,
  matthewjohnsobolev/sirens (stores ukrainealarm data in Postgres but publishes
  no dataset) and a few bots.
  - I found no Kaggle or HuggingFace dataset with levels.
  - I did not check deepstatemap, war.ukrzen or the map.ukrainealarm.com
  internals; unknown.

  **What's verified and what isn't.**
  - Verified: the docs text, SDK code and commits, the dataset issues, and the
  Telegram message contents and ids.
  - Inferred:
    - regionHistory has no levels; this rests on the SDK, not the swagger.
    - `month_ago` probably lacks `threats`.
    - The eTryvoga escalations probably track the official ones; I didn't
  cross-check them against alerts.in.ua.

  **Recommendation.** For history since 7 Sep, scrape eTryvoga. For accurate
  escalation times from now on, log alerts.in.ua `threats[].started_at` or
  ukrainealarm `activeAlertLevels[].createdAt` and webhooks live.

  What it found:
  - Official APIs keep no escalation history.
    - alerts.in.ua shows the time red started (threats[].started_at), but only
      while the alert is active. The history endpoint drops it. Our raw history
      files confirm this: no threats field.

      level.

    no level history. @air_alert_ua's last post was on 7 Sep.
  - One real candidate: the volunteer eTryvoga channel @UkraineAlarmSignal.
    Since 7 Sep it posts a separate timestamped message per raion for yellow,
    red and all-clear. It shows in-place switches: Kharkivskyi raion went yellow
    at 14:56 and red at 15:16 on 11 Oct, with no all-clear in between.
    - Caution: earlier in this project, eTryvoga's start times matched the
      official ones only about 50% of the time. Its escalation times need
      checking against our poller before we trust them.

  against the real escalation times from our poller on 10–11 Oct.

  **Yes, and I'm fairly confident.** **@air_alert_map** ("Карта повітряних
  тривог 🇺🇦") posts a separate, timestamped line for each per-raion or per-city
  yellow→red escalation. Its history covers the whole period since the two-level
  system started (7 Sep). It matched the poller on 44 of 46 overlap
  escalations, with a median lag of +39 s.

  ## Channels checked (read-only Telethon on a *copy* of the session; `lsof`
  showed no other process using the session first; nothing joined or sent)
  - **@air_alert_ua** is silent, which is confirmed. The last post is 2026-09-07
  06:09 UTC (msg 338380). It never used the yellow/red format; its last posts
  are "🔴 HH:MM Повітряна тривога в …" and "🔴🔴 … Підвищений рівень
  небезпеки!".
  - **@air_alert_map** is the candidate (about 20k posts since 25 Aug; history
  goes back to 2022-03-30). Each post is a batch of lines, published every
  minute at about :10–:16 s:
    - New alert: `🟡 [5] Донецький район (25 хв)`. The [n] looks like that day's
  alert count, and the duration looks like the calm gap before it.
    - **Escalation:** `🔴 Донецький район`, with no [n] and no duration.
    - End: `🟢 X (duration)`. There are also `🟢🛸` and `🟢🚀` variants.
    - Cities appear as `м. Харків та Харківська територіальна громада`.
    - The first yellow line is 2026-09-07 10:36 UTC. The channel posts about
  20–97 escalation lines a day from 8 Sep to 11 Oct; 7 Sep has 3.
    - Its "about" still names @air_alert_ua as the data source, which is stale.
  I don't know its real upstream.
  - **@air_alert_mk** (Mykolaiv only) posts levels, e.g. "🟡 Миколаївський район
  — повітряна тривога, жовтий рівень: Дронова загроза (жовтий рівень)". I did
  not check whether it marks escalations.
  - **@kyiv_airraid_alert** (Kyiv only, English) posts "🟡 Drone threat in
  Kyiv!" and "🔴 Missile threat in Kyiv!". I did not check escalations.
  - **@air_alert_telegram, @ukrainar, @airAlarm_Kyiv** are news or
  drone-tracking channels with no structured per-region levels.
  - **@alerts_in_ua** has been inactive since 2025.
  - Web search found only news coverage of the new system (start 6 Sep, Kyiv
  first). No source names a successor to @air_alert_ua.

  ## Validation against the poller (10 Oct 16:17 – 11 Oct 17:12 UTC, `air_raid`
  type, raions and cities only)
  - **Escalations:** 44 of 46 poller yellow→red changes have a matching `🔴 X`
  line. Offsets from the poller's `updated_at` to the post time are min +8 s,
  median +39 s, max +275 s. Example: Донецький район escalated at 17:05:52 per
  the poller and was posted at 17:07:10.
    - Both misses (м. Світловодськ, Харківський район 09:12) are probably name
  or timing edge cases. I did not investigate them.
  - **Escalation lines the poller doesn't have:** 19 of 64 in the window. All
  but one fall in poller gaps; the poller has 1–2 h holes overnight on 10–11
  Oct.
    - In periods the poller fully covers, only 1 of 29 is unmatched: Харківський
  район at 15:35. The channel shows a yellow start, a 0-minute `🟢🛸` and then
  `🔴` in one post. The poller first saw that alert as red with `started_at`
  15:34:19 and `updated_at` 15:34:34, so its roughly 40 s polling missed the
  brief yellow.
  - **De-escalations** (red→yellow): only 1 of 4 match a bare `🟡 X` line, so
  don't rely on that direction.
  - **Alert starts:** 517 of 667 match a bracketed line, median +46 s. Most
  misses look like naming or pairing issues.

  ## Caveats
  - **Verified:**
    - The escalation format exists and matches the poller well on 10–11 Oct.
    - History reaches back to 7 Sep.
    - About 70% of posts since 6 Sep were edited later (median 28 min after
  posting).
  - **Not verified:**
    - Whether those edits change the text or only refresh the map image. The
  tight timing match suggests escalation lines are there when first posted, but
  the API only gives the final text.
    - Accuracy before 10 Oct, since the poller has no data for that period.
    - Whether the per-day `[n]` counter means what I think.
  - The channel time resolution is about 1 minute plus 8–40 s of lag.
  Escalations that happen within seconds of the start show up as start, 0-minute
  end and red together.
  - Names need normalising: `’` vs `'`, and the channel's "м. X та X-ська
  громада" form vs the poller's separate city and hromada records.

✻ Sautéed for 7m 34s · done Sunday 8:14 PM

※ recap: You're building the KSE air-alert forecast, and red forecasts were leaking because history loses when yellow turned red. Next, your call: rebuild red using @air_alert_map's escalation posts (44 of 46 matched the poller), or drop red.

❯ Well, I dont trust this tg thing. what are our options remember               

⏺ Without Telegram, there are four options:

  1. Drop red, keep "any" (my recommendation).
     - Red disappears from the site and CLI; nothing else changes.
     - The README tells the full story: we suspected the escalation leak, built
       a live poller to test it, the poller proved it, and we removed the red
       models.
     - Red had no skill even with the leak, so the product loses nothing real.
       It also makes the strongest "what went wrong" for your reflection.
  2. Redefine red honestly.
     - New question: "Will an alert start that later turns red?" Whether an
       alert ends up red is a future outcome, so using its final level as the
       label is legitimate.
     - Inputs may only use the levels of alerts that had already ended at the
       forecast time. An active alert's level is unknown from history.
     - There is no red-end model, because the switch time is unknown.
     - The red models need retraining, and the E2 test stays untouched. It will
       most likely still show no skill.
  3. Red from our own poller, going forward.
     - Keep logging true switch times live from alerts.in.ua (its
       threats[].started_at).
     - Today that's about one day of data, far too little to train on, so in
       practice this is option 1 now and red returns later. It's worth one line
       in the README as future work.
  4. Keep red with a warning label.
     - The least work, but it knowingly ships a model trained on leaked data.
       I'd avoid it for a submission graded on "no leakage".

  My recommendation is 1, mentioning 3 as future work.

❯ how did you understand it doesn't show transition time for red?               

⏺ I worked it out from the data, not from documentation. One link is still
  inferred, though, so I'll close it with a check.

  Evidence:
  1. The live feed shows the switch. Our poller reads alerts.in.ua's active.json
     about every 40 seconds. Alert 275556 (Donetskyi raion) appeared at 16:20
     as yellow, with updated_at 16:20:03. At 17:06 the same ID showed as red,
     with updated_at 17:05:52 and the same started_at. That's an in-place
     change: no new alert is created, and the poller saw 51 such cases.
  2. History records have nowhere to keep it. Every record in our history files
     (month_ago.json) has a single alert_level field and no threats list, so it
     can hold one level per alert.
  3. updated_at is overwritten at the end. In 15,770 of the 15,936 finished
     history alerts, updated_at equals finished_at within 2 minutes. The
     escalation time that updated_at held while the alert was live is gone once
     it ends.

  What I haven't seen directly: an alert the poller saw escalate, as it appears
  in history afterwards. Our history fetch ran before those alerts happened. So
  "history shows it as red from the start" follows from points 2 and 3, but I
  haven't observed it. I'll fetch Donetsk's history once now and look up 275556
  and the other escalated IDs.

    disappeared.
  - No field records when the switch happened.
  - updated_at now equals the finish time (11 Oct), so the 17:05:52 that was
    there while the alert was live has been overwritten.

  So I'm sure:
  - Live data shows the switch time, but only while the alert is active.
  - History shows only the final level, with no transition time. Any red
    timeline built from history paints red from the yellow start, which is the
    leak.

✻ Crunched for 22s · done Sunday 8:22 PM

❯ if we just keep it as is as "red" does it make sense? I think it does         
  partially                                                                     

⏺ Partly, yes. It depends on which part of "red" you mean.

  What still makes sense as is:
  - Red-start as a target: "an alert starts here that will be red." The final
    level is a real future outcome, so predicting it is legitimate. It just
    answers "will a red-type alert begin within H" rather than "when will the
    siren turn red". For a user that's arguably the more useful question, since
    an escalating alert is a missile threat from its first minute.
  - The "Now" forecast: its inputs come from the live state, so they are honest
    at the moment of forecasting.
  - The scale of the problem: about 16% of red alerts (51 of 325) escalated in
    place, after a median of about 40 minutes. Most red data is correct.

  What doesn't:
  - Red inputs during training and scoring. "Red now", "neighbours red now" and
    "oblasts red nationally" treat an escalating alert as red during its yellow
    phase. The model learned from, and was scored with, information nobody had
    at that moment. Live, those inputs will be lower than in training, so
    validation looks better than reality.
  - Red-end. "When does red end" needs the real red start, so a red alert's
    duration so far is overstated by the hidden yellow phase. This model is the
    most contaminated.

  A middle ground (a lighter version of option 2):
  1. Keep red-start, renamed honestly: "a red-level alert starts". Its label
     stays as is.
  2. Build its red inputs only from alerts that had already ended by the
     forecast time. Once an alert has ended, its final level is history, not a
     peek ahead. The any-inputs stay as they are.
  3. Drop red-end and say why in the README.
  4. Retrain red-start only and compare with the current one on validation. If
     the leaky version scored better, the gap measures how much the leak was
     inflating it.

  That keeps red in the product, removes the leak, and turns the finding into a
  measured result instead of a caveat. It means changing how the red inputs are
  built and retraining one model; the E2 test stays untouched. If red-start
  still shows no skill, it stays labelled "no better than historical rates", as
  now.

❯ Let's get back to tg channels. I think it could be accurate, this data is     
  important to have. Let me know all caveats with backfilling transitions data  

  Ran 2 shell commands (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ Here are the caveats for backfilling yellow→red switch times from
  @air_alert_map. A few of them I measured on the full dump just now.

  Trust in the source
  1. Unofficial, with an unknown upstream. Its "about" text still names
     @air_alert_ua, which went silent on 7 Sep. It most likely mirrors
     alerts.in.ua or ukrainealarm, but that isn't confirmed.
  2. Checked on only about one day. It matched 44 of 46 real switches on 10–11
     Oct, the only window where the poller gives ground truth. September can't
     be checked directly, and the yellow/red criteria were adjusted week to
     week. On 7 Sep, its first day, it has only 3 escalation lines.

  Edits (the biggest leakage question)

  3. 70% of posts since 7 Sep were edited later, a median of 29 minutes after
     posting; 8,701 were edited more than 5 minutes later. We only see the final
     text.
     - Reassuring: on 10–11 Oct no 🔴 line was dated before the real switch; the
       earliest came 8 seconds after it. So in that window, edits did not add
       switches that hadn't happened yet. The edits are probably map-image
       refreshes.
     - Not proven: we can't rule this out for September.
  4. Deleted posts are invisible.

  What the lines mean (inferred, not documented)

  5. There are four kinds of red/yellow lines:
     - 🔴 [n] X (…) (3,625): an alert that starts as red;
     - 🔴 X with no duration (1,814) and 🔴 X (20 хв) with a duration (1,623).
       Two forms of escalation line, and I don't know what separates them. The
       duration may be how long it was yellow.
     - 🟡 X (…) with no counter (3,371): probably red→yellow downgrades. Those
       matched the poller only 1 of 4 times, so they're unreliable.

     These need checking against the poller before we rely on them.

  Matching to our data

  6. Names:
     - apostrophe variants;
     - cities written as "м. X та X-ська громада";
     - oblast-wide declarations in history vs. per-raion lines in the channel.

     Some red alerts won't match any line.
  7. Unmatched red alerts need a rule. For a red alert with no escalation or
     red-start line, we can treat it as red from the start (a small leftover
     leak) or mark its red timing as unknown and exclude it. The share of
     unmatched alerts decides how much this matters, and it isn't measured yet.
  8. Conflicts between the two sources: a channel escalation could fall outside
     the alerts.in.ua alert's time span.

  Timing

  9. Red starts late, which is the safe direction. The channel posts in roughly
     one-minute batches, about 40 seconds after the switch (up to 4.5 minutes).
     Red is never marked early, so no leak, but red durations come out slightly
     short. A yellow phase of a few seconds appears as a start, a 0-minute end
     and red all in one post.
  10. No outages: there is no posting gap longer than 3 hours since 7 Sep.

  Product and submission

  11. Training and live use must define red the same way. Training would use
      channel-derived red, while the live forecast uses the alerts.in.ua level.
      These agree to within about 40 seconds on escalations.
  12. Reproducibility: a reviewer needs a Telegram account to re-pull the
      channel. I'd commit the small derived escalation table and the pull
      script, not the raw dump.

  If you go ahead, I'd gate it on three checks:
  - Coverage: what share of September–October red alerts in history match a
    channel line, per oblast. If too many are missing, red stays partly
    contaminated.
  - Poller cross-check: rerun the comparison over all overlap days, including
    what each line form means. The poller keeps running and adds days for free.
  - Leakage test: extend the property test so that red inputs at time t never
    use a channel line dated after t.

  Then retrain only the red models, compare with the current leaky versions on
  validation, and keep the E2 test untouched.

⏺ Updated plan
