⏺ User approved Claude's plan
  ⎿  Plan saved to: ~/.claude/plans/snoopy-skipping-cake.md · /plan to edit
     Fix the red-level leak: backfill yellow→red switch times from 
     @air_alert_map

     Context

     The leakage audit found, and a re-fetch of Donetsk history confirmed, the
     following:
     - alerts.in.ua escalates alerts in place (same id, same started_at).
     - Its history keeps only the final alert_level, and updated_at is
       overwritten at finish.
     - So every escalated alert is painted red from its yellow start
       (timeline.build_timeline, the red _paint). The red models therefore
       trained and were validated on red that "starts" before it was declared.
     - The any-models are unaffected: they use no red inputs, and the any state
       ignores level.

     No official source keeps switch times. The unofficial channel
     @air_alert_map posts a separate 🔴 <raion> line for each in-place
     escalation, from 7 Sep onwards.
     - Against the live poller (10–11 Oct) it matched 44 of 46 switches.
     - It always posts after the real switch (median +39 s), so using post time
       can only make red late, never early.

     Bohdan doesn't fully trust it, so the plan is: use it as the source, and
     prove it first with three independent checks. Proceed only if they pass;
     otherwise fall back to an "honest red-start" version that uses only levels
     of alerts that have already ended.

     Approach

     1. Pull the channel (reproducible, append-only)

     - src/alerts/escalations.py, pull(raw_dir): Telethon read-only
       iter_messages on air_alert_map from 2026-09-06.
       - Writes data/raw/air_alert_map_<stamp>.jsonl with {id, date, edit, 
         text}.
       - Append-only, the same as the alerts.in.ua store.
       - Credentials come from env (TELEGRAM_API_ID, TELEGRAM_API_HASH,
         TELEGRAM_SESSION path). Locally they point to a temporary copy of the
         telegram skill's session, which is deleted after use. Never join or
         send.
     - CLI: alerts fetch --channel (a new flag on the existing fetch).
     - Dependency: add telethon to pyproject.toml.
     - Seed the store with the dump already pulled (25 Aug – 11 Oct, now in the
       scratchpad).

     2. Parse the posts and attach red start times (escalations.py)

     - parse(paths) -> DataFrame[posted_at, mark, counter, place, duration].
       - The line regex comes from the validated
         scratchpad/escalation/validate2.py.
       - Name normalisation: ’→', and "м. X та …громада"→"м. X".
       - Duplicates are removed by message id.
     - red_onsets(events, lines) -> Series red_from, for alerts.in.ua rows with
       level == "red":
       - a bracketed 🔴 [n] <place> line within −1/+5 min of started_at →
         red_from = started_at (red from the start);
       - otherwise, the first unbracketed 🔴 <place> line inside [started_at, 
         finished_at] → red_from = posted_at (escalation);
       - otherwise unmatched → red_from = NaT, so the alert is treated as not 
         red. This is conservative: never early, and it slightly undercounts
         red. Counts are reported.
       - Oblast-level red declarations: match any line in a raion of that
         oblast. The rule is finalised from the coverage numbers in step 4.
       - Known limit, documented: red→yellow downgrades are ignored, because the
         history's final level hides them. Only 1 was seen in the poller
         window.

     3. Use it in the timeline

     - sources.load_alerts_in_ua: unchanged.
     - timeline.load:
       - reads the channel store and adds a red_from column to events (NaT for
         non-red rows and for official rows);
       - if the store is missing, it raises an error instead of silently falling
         back to the leaky definition.
     - timeline.build_timeline: the red stream is painted from red_from, not
       started_at.
     - timeline.truncate: a red_from after the cutoff becomes NaT, so the alert
       is not red yet at the cutoff.
     - The "Now" forecast keeps using the live state, consistent within the ~40
       s lag.

     4. Gates (run before any retraining; numbers go into 
     reports/escalations.json and the README)

     - alerts check-escalations (new CLI command):
       - (a) Coverage: the share of red alerts.in.ua alerts (10 Sep – 1 Oct, the
         train and validation window) that get a red_from, per oblast and
         overall.
       - (b) Poller: for every in-place escalation in
         data/raw/active_poll.jsonl, the derived red_from minus the true switch
         time (updated_at of the first red snapshot). Report the match rate and
         offsets, and check that no switch is ever earlier than the truth.
       - (c) Official cross-check for September: for Kyiv oblast, Vinnytsia,
         Kherson and Rivne (whose administration channels post per-raion 🔴 … 
         червоний рівень messages), compare their escalation messages with
         @air_alert_map's. Reuse the pulls in scratchpad/official/, re-pulled
         through the same pull() with a channel list. Report agreement within 3
         min.
     - Proceed only if all hold:
       - coverage ≥ 90%;
       - poller match ≥ 90%, with no early switches;
       - official agreement ≥ 85% where both posted.
     - Otherwise, stop and report to Bohdan. The fallback is the honest
       red-start version.

     5. Retrain red only, re-validate, keep test sealed

     - evaluate.train / alerts train: add a --kinds red-start,red-end option so
       the any-models stay frozen. Their artifacts and E1 results are untouched.
     - Before retraining, save the current red validation metrics as
       reports/e2/val_metrics_leaky_red.json for a before/after comparison. The
       drop measures how much the leak was inflating red.
     - E2 test: still untouched. Monday's single run uses the fixed red.

     6. Tests, docs, site

     - tests/test_leakage.py:
       - the existing truncation test also covers red_from (cutoffs inside a
         yellow phase that escalates later);
       - new test: red_from >= started_at, and red_from >= posted_at of its
         line, never earlier.
     - README:
       - add @air_alert_map to the Data sources table;
       - rewrite the Leakage paragraph on red escalation: suspected, poller
         built, poller proved it, backfill, gate results, before/after red
         skill;
       - list the caveats in Limits: unofficial source, final text only after
         edits, downgrades ignored, unmatched alerts treated as not red;
       - update the Monday steps.
     - Site: update the red note text only.
     - Vault: the KSE page gets the leak finding and @air_alert_map as the
       escalation source (with gate numbers). Add an ingest ledger line.
     - Commit and push.

     Files

     - New: src/alerts/escalations.py.
     - Changed:
       - src/alerts/timeline.py (load, build_timeline, truncate);
       - src/alerts/cli.py (fetch --channel, check-escalations, train --kinds);
       - src/alerts/evaluate.py (train kinds filter);
       - pyproject.toml;
       - tests/test_leakage.py;
       - README.md;
       - assets/site.html.
     - Reused:

  The bug (fixed and pushed, 77c6b77):
  - Cause: the first fetch on 10 Oct also saved files for Luhansk, Crimea and
    one more region we don't use. The code takes the data end as the oldest
    "last fetched" time across all region files. Those three were never
    refetched, so the data end stayed at 10 Oct 18:47 Kyiv time, and "Now"
    showed alerts from then.

    it.

    dates before 10 Oct.

  How updates work:
  - The site never refreshes itself. It shows the state at the last fetch, and
    the header says "Forecast made at … Kyiv time".
  - To update it, run alerts fetch then alerts report. alerts.in.ua allows 2
    requests per minute and we need one per region, so a fetch takes about 12
    minutes; that's the floor.
  - The final fetch is tomorrow, before the test run, so the submitted site will
    show Monday's state.

  A near-live "Now" is possible: alerts.in.ua's active-alerts endpoint returns
  the current state of the whole country in one request. That needs a running
  server or a scheduled rebuild, though, and the task doesn't ask for
  deployment, so I'd skip it unless you want it for the demo.

⏺ The site now updates the latest alert status every minute. The change is
  committed locally (eb6a2d1) and not pushed; I'll push only when you say yes.
  I've saved that rule to memory.

  How it works
  - New command, alerts live: once a minute it asks alerts.in.ua for the alerts
    active right now (one request), adds the snapshot to the poller log, and
    recomputes the "Now" forecast. One tick takes about 1.2 seconds. It writes
    the result to site/live.json.
  - The page fetches live.json every minute and redraws the "Now" map, the Kyiv
    callout and the curve. The status line reads "live · polled 22:46".
  - Between full fetches, the poller log fills in everything since the last full
    history download: alerts that started or ended, and the exact yellow→red
    switch times seen live.
  - The old 40-second poller is stopped. alerts live replaces it and keeps
    writing to the same log, so the escalation evidence keeps growing.

  Running now: the live loop and the local server, at http://127.0.0.1:8000/. My
  test screenshot shows forecast time 22:45 Kyiv and Kyiv quiet, matching what
  you see.

  Limits:
  - It's live only while alerts live runs on this machine. Opening index.html as
    a file, or from GitHub, shows the snapshot from build time.
  - If the loop is stopped for a while, any alert that both started and ended in
    that gap is missed. Restarting it picks up from there.

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ Done: anything above 99% now shows as ">99%" and anything below 1% as "<1%",
  on the map labels, the curve and the tooltips. Reload the page to see it. It's
  committed locally (9d0b0fa), not pushed.

  On your question: no, the forecast doesn't learn from the whole war. Here's
  what it trains on:

  Model: any-start, any-end
  Training data: Dec 2025 – 28 Aug 2026 (old system) + 13–27 Sep 2026 (new
  system), with a flag marking which system each row is from
  Why: the old data adds 9 months of patterns; the flag lets the model learn how

  the new system differs
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Model: red-start, red-end
  Training data: 13–27 Sep 2026 only
  Why: red only exists since 6 Sep

  Why not 2022–2025:
  - The meaning of "alert" keeps changing. In December 2025 declarations moved
    to raion level. In September 2026 the yellow/red split arrived, after which
    Kyiv City went from about 6% to about 32% of the time under alert. Data from
    2022 describes a different event with different frequencies.
  - The threats changed. Drone swarm volumes, missile tactics and frontline
    geography in 2022–2023 don't look like today's. Older patterns would teach
    the model things that are no longer true.
  - Recent data proved worth more. On validation, the model trained only on
    old-system data got worse at longer horizons (−19% at 24 h). Data from even
    a few months back already transfers poorly, and 2022 would be worse.

  So it's deliberately a forecast for today's alert system. It learns daily
  rhythms, neighbour spread and wave patterns from the last 9 months, and how
  often alerts happen now from the last few weeks.

  Kyiv's ">99% within 24 h" comes from that recent window: under the new system
  Kyiv City has never gone a full day without an alert. That number would only
  drop if the system or the attack pattern changed again.
✻ Worked for 22s · done Sunday 10:50 PM

❯ is this how it should be?                                                     

⏺ Mostly yes. It's the right design for this task, with one part that is
  reasoned but not measured, and one real limit.

  Why it fits:
  - The task says "use the most recent data, evaluate on the latest period".
    Training on the current alert system and testing on the newest days is
    exactly that.
  - Forecasting standard: when a process changes its rules, as Ukraine's alerts
    did in Dec 2025 and Sep 2026, the usual practice is to weight recent data
    and flag the change. That's what the regime flag does.
  - Measured: old + new data beats new-only and old-only on validation. Adding
    the 9 months of old data was tested, and it helps.

  What I haven't proven: that adding 2022–2025 would hurt. That rests on
  reasoning (definition changes, different tactics) and indirect evidence
  (old-only collapses at 24 h), not on a direct test. If you want it settled, I
  can run one more ablation: train with 2023–2025 added, compare on validation
  only, and leave the test untouched. Roughly an hour of compute. It would also
  be a good process point for the AI log: "we tested the assumption instead of
  asserting it".

  The real limit: the model is frozen as of 27 Sep, with only about 2 weeks of
  new-system training. Kyiv's ">99% within 24 h" is right for how the last month
  went. But the General Staff adjusts the yellow/red criteria weekly, and a
  real deployed version would retrain weekly. For the submission, a frozen model
  is correct (no peeking at test). As a product, I'd list weekly retraining as
  the first next step, which the README already mentions as a limit.

  So: sound for the submission. If you want the 2023–2025 assumption checked
  rather than argued, say so and I'll run that ablation.

✻ Brewed for 10s · done Sunday 10:51 PM

❯ what's new-system exactly and why does it start from 27 sep                   

⏺ The new system is the two-level alert system Ukraine switched to on 6 
  September 2026:
  - Yellow: a drone threat. These are frequent; about 63% of air-raid alerts are
    now yellow.
  - Red: a missile, ballistic or massed attack.

  Before that, the old system had one siren-backed alert, declared much less
  often. That's why Kyiv City went from about 6% to about 32% of the time under
  alert. "Old system" and "new system" in the project mean before and after that
  switch.

  The 27 Sep date is where new-system training ends, not where it starts. The
  new-system data is split by time like this:

  ┌────────────┬────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │   Period   │                            Use                             │
  ├────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │            │ missing: the official CSV stopped on 7 Sep, and            │
  │ 6–10 Sep   │ alerts.in.ua history only goes back 30 days from our first │
  │            │  fetch (10 Oct), so it starts 10 Sep                       │
  ├────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ 10–13 Sep  │ warm-up: features need 72 h of history, so these days feed │
  │            │  inputs but aren't training rows                           │
  ├────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ 13–27 Sep  │ training (about 2 weeks)                                   │
  ├────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ 28 Sep – 1 │ validation: picking the champion model and tuning          │
  │  Oct       │                                                            │
  ├────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ 2 Oct →    │ test: the model never sees it, and it's evaluated once     │
  │ final      │ tomorrow                                                   │
  │ fetch      │                                                            │
  └────────────┴────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

  So of about 5 weeks of new-system data, 2 go to training, 4 days to
  validation, and the rest is held back as the honest test of "the latest
  period", as the task requires. Training on more of it would mean testing on
  less, or on nothing recent.

  The old-system rows (Dec 2025 – Aug 2026) come along in training only for the
  any-models, flagged so the model knows which system each row comes from.
